## 100. S0 v3.2: Wind Interconnection Price, Storage Credit Phase-Down, WA Two-Tranche (Flags); CA Tranche and p1 Solar Proposals

**Date:** 2026-10-11. **Branch:** `tom/s0-v3.2`, from tag `s0-v3.1` (3160be1). The credits branch keeps §95–§99; this
entry is numbered after them so the two branches merge without a clash.

**Defaults:** every flag defaults to the v3.1 behaviour, so a v3.1 case builds the same inputs until Tom approves.

**Untouched:** the build-rate module (decisions R1–R11 pending) and the DOE levers.

**Write-up:** `Guides and documentation/s0_v3.2.md`.

**Brought in:**
- From d51c68f: `interconnection_headroom/docs/wind_price_check.md` and `scripts/wind_price_check.py` (§99).
- From 6adc228, the §90 credit writer: `s0_workflow/tax_credits.py`, `credit_tally.py`, `scripts/credit_tally.py` and
  the `gen_tax_credits_file` hook. It is inert for S0: no production terms are set, and `gen_tax_credits.csv` is
  byte-identical to the s0-v3.1 writer's.
- `s0_workflow/scripts/check_vre_profiles.py` and its test.
- Not brought in: §90's S-set settings and tests (they stay on the credits branch).

**a. Wind interconnection price factor.**
- **Flag:** `interconnection_headroom.price_factors` (`config.yaml` `saturation.price_factors: {wind: null}`).
- **What it does:** with a factor f set, wind keeps its 0.25 room weight but pays f × the step price per MW of
  nameplate, through its own share of each step and of the release (`ICStepPriced`, `ICReleasePriced` in
  `study_modules.interconnection_headroom`). The share adds (f / w − 1) × the step price on top.
- **Files:** `switch_case.py` writes `ic_price_factors.csv` only when f is set. A factor below the weight stops the
  build.
- **Outputs:** `priced_*` columns.
- **Candidate:** 1.0, with sensitivities 0.8 and 1.3.
- **Tests:** a toy run at none, 0.8, 1.0 and 1.3 (steps fill in cost order; wind pays f × the step price per MW),
  plus writer tests (no file by default, the other files unchanged, low factor refused).

**b. Battery storage credit with the OBBBA phase-down.**
- **Flag:** `s0_production.storage_credit.mode`: `flat` (v3.1, ×0.7 in every period) or `obbba`.
- **What `obbba` does:** the `tax_credits` axis's ×0.7 becomes ×1.0, and a 30% 48E investment term goes to the credit
  writer (`kind: investment`).
- **Effect:** new-build battery capex becomes × (1 − 0.30 × phase), where the phase is 1 through 2033, then 0.75
  (2034), 0.5 (2035) and 0 (from 2036). The `phase_basis` setting chooses `period_label` or `span_mean`. The build
  writes `investment_credit_report.csv`.
- **Battery capex by stage, × ATB:**
  - flat: 0.70 in every stage;
  - obbba, label: 0.70 / 0.70 / 0.85 / 1.00 / 1.00;
  - obbba, span mean: 0.70 / 0.70 / 0.745 / 1.00 / 1.00;
  - add ×1.2 for the tariff.
- **Tests:** in `test_storage_credit.py`. The regression-row resolution test now lists the flag at its inert value.

**c. WA import charge, two-tranche.**
- **Flag:** `ca_wa_carbon.wa_import_charge`: `per_mwh` (v3.1) or `two_tranche`.
- **What it does:** `study_modules.trans_hurdle_cost` gains named tranches (`trans_import_tranches*.csv`) in the CA
  LP form, costed in `TxImportTrancheCost`. WA's border imports go to tranche "WA": free `wa_two_tranche.free_mwh_per_yr`,
  then price × 0.437. CA's files are unchanged.
- **Equivalence:** free 0 reproduces v3.1 (toy test).
- **Proposed value:** a charged block equal to WA's unspecified volume, 13.2 TWh/yr. The source is the Commerce
  CY2023 fuel mix: unspecified 14.85% of 89.14 TWh, read from search results, so check it against the report. The
  free tranche = WA's modelled 2028 imports − 13.2 TWh, which needs the runner's 2028 WA import flow.

**d. CA free tranche (proposal).**
- **Source:** MRR's specified/unspecified split is the charge's own basis. CEC's TSEG counts firmed-and-shaped
  renewables as imports.
- **Count:** large hydro, geothermal and biomass.
- **Trajectory:** flat to 2030, then scaled by an exogenous WECC-ex-CA clean-generation index. The endogenous α form
  is a sensitivity.
- **Candidates (2028 CA imports sit at the kink in S0 v3.1, so they follow the tranche):**
  - D1 49.4 TWh (v3.1);
  - D2 52.7 TWh (+ geothermal and biomass; recommended);
  - D3 about 39 TWh (MRR-indicative; pending CARB data).

**e. p1 (WA) solar (proposal).**
- **Finding:** p1's new-build PV cluster profiles diverge from each other, from p1's existing PV and from the other
  WA zones, by a different amount each weather year (cluster 1 0.21–0.87 of peers, cluster 2 0.14–0.46). There is no
  time-zone shift and no missing data.
- **Likely cause:** profile rows joined to the wrong reV cells in most weather years, by position instead of gid.
- **Test and fix:** compare per-cell yearly mean CF with the supply-curve `mean_cf` on the VM, then rebuild the p1
  profiles with a gid join.
- **Weather years:** S0 uses all seven (2007–2013).

## 109. S0 v3.2: `make_generator_variability` No Longer Cached

**Date:** 2026-10-11. **Branch:** `tom/s0-v3.2` only.

**The change:** `pg_to_switch.py` called PowerGenome's `make_generator_variability` through a joblib cache
(`memory.cache`, the `cache/` folder). It now calls the function directly. `kmeans_time_clustering` keeps its cache.

**Why (the runner's measurements):**
- The cache saved about 2.3 s per stage but wrote about 6.5 GB per build.
- No entry was ever reused. The key hashes the whole generator table, including columns that never reach the written
  files (credit settings, for example). So S4 and S2, whose 2028 inputs are byte-identical, got different keys.

**Inputs unchanged:**
- A joblib cache only memoises. A hit returns the stored output of an identical call, and a miss runs the function.
  So calling the function directly gives the same `variable_capacity_factors.csv` and every other file. The call
  itself (`make_generator_variability(period_gens)`) is unchanged.
- This couldn't be checked by a build here (no PowerGenome data in the cloud).
- **Runner check:** every v3.2 flag defaults to v3.1, so a v3.2 build of S0prod_A 2028 should hash identically to the
  archived s0-v3.1 2028 inputs (`SHA256SUMS.txt`).

**Clean-up:** existing cache entries for the function, under `cache/joblib/` in folders named after
`make_generator_variability`, can be deleted. Nothing reads them now.

**Test:** `s0_workflow/tests/test_variability_cache.py` checks that the function isn't wrapped by the cache, that the
single call site uses it directly, and that `kmeans_time_clustering` is still cached.

## 110. S0 v3.2: Read-Once Input Cache for the Case Build

**Date:** 2026-10-11. **Branch:** `tom/s0-v3.2` only.

**The runner's finding:** a 5-stage BILL central build takes about 83 min. Most of it goes to hourly demand (about
22 min) and PowerGenome flexible demand (about 20 min), then generator post-processing (about 11 min) and
existing-generator clustering (about 10 min). Generator profiles and time sampling take about 20 s per stage.

**What repeats (from `pg_to_switch.py`):** `main` builds all stages in one process, one loop over `to_run`.
- Each stage calls PowerGenome's `make_final_load_curves`, which reads the hourly load file (`regional_load_fn`).
- Each stage builds the flexible-demand resources, which read `demand_response_fn` once per resource type.
  pg_to_switch's own aggregate-zone fix reads the same file again for each type.
- `GeneratorClusters` reads the EIA-860M workbook (`eia_860m_fn`), the coal tracker (`GEM_coal_tracker_fn`) and the
  DG profiles (`distributed_gen_fn`).
- None of these files depends on the stage year. Only what is done with them does.
- Our own per-stage reads (coal tables, hierarchy, headroom tables, growth caps) are small committed files, each
  under 300 KB. Nothing year-independent and heavy turned up in pg_to_switch's steps 3–4 besides those PowerGenome
  reads.

**Change:** a new module, `s0_workflow/read_once.py`. pg_to_switch wraps the stage loop in
`read_once(input_file_names(case_settings))`.
- **Scope:** inside the block, `pandas.read_csv`, `read_parquet` and `read_excel` calls on a file named by any `*_fn`
  setting are parsed once and then served from memory.
- **Key:** the resolved path, size, mtime, reader and all call arguments.
- **Copies:** every call gets a deep copy, so in-place edits don't leak between stages.
- **Bypassed (go straight to pandas):** buffers, file handles, `chunksize` / `iterator`, callable arguments, and files
  that aren't named.
- **No disk cache.** The cache is cleared at the end of the build.
- **Turning it off:** `PG_READ_ONCE=0`.
- **Build log:** a summary line per file (reads, parses, parse seconds, estimated seconds saved).

**Byte-identical inputs:** `test_read_once.py` runs a five-stage stand-in. Each stage reads and edits in place the
load and DR zips (by position and by keyword) and an 860M-style workbook (one sheet, and all sheets), then writes
files.
- All 30 written files are byte-identical with and without the cache.
- Each file is parsed once (the workbook once per distinct call), and the rest are served from memory.
- A cached frame equals pandas' own read exactly: values, dtypes, index and columns.
- A changed file is re-read.
- pandas is restored afterwards, even after an error.
- The build loop is checked to run inside the block.

**Not verified here (no PowerGenome or input data in the cloud; the PowerGenome clone is off limits):**
- whether PowerGenome reads these files through `pd.read_csv` / `read_excel` / `read_parquet`, rather than
  `ExcelFile`, pyarrow or polars directly, or a `from pandas import read_csv` bound at import;
- how much of the 22 + 20 min is parsing, as opposed to per-year processing, which still runs every stage.

The build log's read-once line shows both on the VM: no line, or zero memory hits, means PowerGenome reads another
way. So the 30 min target is not confirmed.

**Memory:** the parsed load file stays in memory for the whole build, and each read makes a transient copy. If memory
is tight, set `PG_READ_ONCE=0`.

**Runner check:** build BILL central (or S0prod_A) with and without `PG_READ_ONCE=0` and compare `SHA256SUMS` of every
stage folder. Then report the timing and the read-once log line.

## 111. S0 v3.2: Zeroed reV Solar Profiles: Neighbour-Fill Repair (Flag), Re-Extraction Route, CF-Basis Re-Assessment

**Date:** 2026-10-11. **Branch:** `tom/s0-v3.2` only. Numbered §111 because §109 and §110 are already taken on this
branch.

**The finding (dashboards session):**
- In `solar_rev_profiles_20240801.parquet` (RESOURCE_GROUP_PROFILES), 5,390 reV sites (about 8%; blocks of 539 every
  6,468 columns) are all zero in weather years 2007–2011 and normal in 2012–13. Wind is unaffected.
- Model PV CF is understated in 101 of 134 zones: p1 30%, p5 24%, p11 21%, p14 18%, p26 and p79 about 12%, and 1–8%
  elsewhere.
- Data: branch `data/rev-cf-mismatch` (`rev_cf_mismatch_118.csv`; `zeroed_pv_sites.csv` and `zones_affected.csv` to
  follow).

**1. Repair behind a flag (`s0_production.pv_profile_repair.mode`: off (default, v3.1) or `neighbour_fill`).**

`s0_workflow/scripts/repair_pv_profiles.py` (library `s0_workflow/pv_profile_repair.py`) writes a repaired copy of the
profiles folder. The source folder is only read.
- **Fill rule:** each all-zero site-year of a site that has output in other years is filled from the nearest unaffected
  site (great circle) in the same weather year, so the alignment with load and wind is kept. The fill is scaled by the
  ratio of the two sites' 2012–13 means and clipped to [0, 1]; clipped hours are counted.
- **Batches:** the file is processed in row batches (pyarrow) and never loaded whole. The output keeps the schema,
  column order and non-site columns.
- **Repaired folder:** the repaired file under its original name, hard links (else copies) of the folder's other files,
  `pv_profile_repair_log.csv` (one row per zeroed site-year: neighbour, distance, scale, clipped hours, status) and
  `pv_profile_repair_manifest.json`.
- **Sites table:** `--sites` (site, latitude, longitude, capacity_mw), or built from the CPA metadata and site mapping.

**The build with `neighbour_fill`** (`production.apply_pv_profile_repair`):
- It checks the manifest: the file must be unchanged since the repair, and no site-year with capacity may still be all
  zero. With `verify: full` and the sites table, it rescans the file instead. Either failure stops the build.
- It then points `RESOURCE_GROUP_PROFILES` at the folder. PowerGenome takes that setting from `pg/settings/env.yml`
  (`pg_data.yml`).
- It copies the fill log into each case folder.
- With the flag off, a repaired folder in `RESOURCE_GROUP_PROFILES` is refused, so the two can't be mixed up.

**Test:** `test_pv_profile_repair.py`, on hand-built profiles. It checks:
- the nearest neighbour, the same-year fill and the scale;
- reference years and unaffected columns unchanged;
- results independent of batch size and row groups;
- a site with capacity that is zero in every year fails the check;
- clipping is counted;
- the build flag's on, off, mismatch and changed-file cases.

**2. Re-extraction (proposal).**
- **Source of the profiles:** Greg Schivley produced them in May 2025 by running reV on the Princeton sites with NSRDB
  weather (`pg_data.yml`).
- **Proper fix:** regenerate 2007–2011 for the 5,390 sites. NREL publishes the reV utility-PV supply curves with hourly
  profiles for about 60,000 sites on OEDI: submission 6001 (2023), representative profiles for 1998–2023, and the 2024
  ReEDS hourly CF dataset for 2007–13 and 2016–23.
- **NSRDB weather:** on AWS Open Data (`s3://nrel-pds-nsrdb`, public, `--no-sign-request`).
- **Needed from the VM:**
  - the parquet's column ids and `solar_site_mapping_20240801.parquet`, to tie columns to reV `sc_point_gid`;
  - the 2012–13 means of about 20 affected and 20 unaffected sites, to check that an OEDI profile reproduces this file's
    reV configuration (tracking, DC/AC ratio, losses) before substituting it;
  - the PowerGenome data provenance for 20240801 (Greg or the PowerGenome team), since a re-export of 2007–2011 from the
    original run is the cleanest route.
- Neither OEDI nor NSRDB was reachable from the cloud session, so none of this was tried.

**3. Re-assessing the reV CF basis** (`s0_workflow/scripts/rev_cf_reassess.py` on the 118 list). Model PV CF divided by
(1 − the zone's understatement):

| Zone | Before | After |
|---|---|---|
| p11 | 0.77 | 0.98 |
| p26 | 0.89 | 1.01 |
| p79 | 0.76 | 0.86 |
| p5 | 0.61 | 0.81 |
| p1 | 0.54 | 0.77 (0.177 vs reV 0.232) |

- p14, with an 18% understatement, isn't in the list, so its corrected ratio is above about 1.03.
- **No uniform AC/DC basis difference.** p1's residual 0.77 matches 1/1.3 (DC vs AC with an inverter loading ratio of
  1.3). But a basis difference would apply to every zone, and p11, p26 and p14 reconcile fully once corrected.
- **Still open, until `zones_affected.csv` arrives:** the residual in p1, p5 and the PJM zones (p125 0.69, p122 0.76,
  p124 0.80, whose shares aren't quoted).
- **Next checks for p1:** its share on the sampled days rather than the full record (S0 samples all seven years; five
  are zeroed), and the model CF over the full record instead of the sampled hours.
- **Wind:** unchanged by this finding (onshore median 0.92, floating offshore 0.81). The hub-height and losses question
  stays open.

**Update with the dashboards' data** (`data/rev-cf-mismatch` 9383b09; copied to `s0_workflow/data/pv_profile_repair/`:
`zeroed_pv_sites.csv`, `zones_affected.csv`, `README_zeroed_pv_profiles.md`, `rev_cf_mismatch_118.csv`):
- **What's zeroed:** 68,923 site columns (`0`–`68922`, the `Site` of `solar_site_mapping_20240801.parquet`; no reV gid in
  the file). 5,390 columns in 10 blocks of 539 are zero for whole years, but not the same years: block 0 is zero in
  2007–2011, blocks 1–2 in 2007, 2010 and 2011, block 3 in 2007 and 2009–2011, blocks 4–9 in 2010–2011. In total that
  is 14,553 site-years.
- **Missing data, not mismatched sites:** re-matching by ID can't recover these years.
- **p1:** its damage sits in cluster 2 (81 zeroed sites, 32.0 GW eligible).

**Repair changes for this data:**
- **Per-year neighbours:** the neighbour is now the nearest site with data in that weather year and in 2012–13, so a
  site zero only in 2010–11 can fill a block 0 site's 2007–2009. Before, only sites with data in every year counted.
- **Metadata defaults:** the sites table is built from `solar_lcoe_ReEDS.csv` and the site mapping, with columns
  `CPA_ID`, `Site`, `latitude`, `longitude` and `cpa_mw` matched case-insensitively. A missing column stops the build
  and lists the columns there are.
- **Cross-check:** `--zeroed` compares the fills with `zeroed_pv_sites.csv`; the result goes in the manifest.
- **Fill quality:** the manifest records distance quantiles, the number of distinct neighbours, the most site-years
  filled from one neighbour and the scale range. Fills more than 100 km away are warned (`--warn-km`), because the
  blocks are contiguous columns and may be contiguous areas: p1's cluster 2 could be filled from distant edge sites.
- **Test:** `test_neighbour_is_chosen_per_weather_year`.

**Re-assessment with `zones_affected.csv`** (`rev_cf_reassess_118.csv`):
- **The 27 PV pairs:** after dividing by (1 − understatement), 19 of 27 are inside 0.85–1.15 (before: 0), with a
  median of 0.88 and p11 0.98, p26 1.02.
- **A small common gap:** across all 101 affected zones, the profile CF over years with data is a median 0.95 of the
  supply-curve CF (IQR 0.92–0.98). That points to a small, common basis or losses difference (about 5%). It is not
  the 23% that an AC/DC basis (1/1.3) would give.
- **p1 and p5 stay outliers:** 0.77 (0.178 against 0.231) and 0.84 on full-record years with data. Sampling isn't the
  cause in p1: the modelled CF over the full record (0.125) equals the CF on the sampled hours (0.124).
- **Next check for p1 and p5:** each site's per-year mean against `cf_years_with_data`, for partial-year gaps that the
  whole-year test misses, plus the 2012–13 means of p1's block 0 sites, whose only years with data are those two.
- **The PJM gaps aren't from zeroed sites:** p125 (0.69), p122 (0.76) and p124 (0.80) aren't among the 101 zones. They
  need the full-record profile CF of those zones from the dashboards session, to separate day sampling from the
  profiles.

## 112. S0 v3.2: Sample-Fidelity Option (Proposal), Time Alignment Notes, p1 CF-Basis Check

**Date:** 2026-10-11. **Branch:** `tom/s0-v3.2`. **Status:** proposals only, nothing implemented; the repair (§111)
stays as planned.

**Data:** `data/rev-cf-mismatch` fd4a3a1, copied to `s0_workflow/data/pv_profile_repair/`:
`README_cf_basis_checks.md`, `pv_site_years_p1_p5.csv`, `p1_block0_vs_neighbours.csv` and `pjm_cf_basis.csv`.

**Repair confirmed:**
- There are no partial-year gaps.
- p1's 91 block-0 sites have 2012–13 CF at 0.98 of their unaffected neighbours'.
- Those neighbours' 2007–11 CF is 1.03× their 2012–13 level. So filling the same weather year from a neighbour, scaled
  by the 2012–13 ratio, keeps that year-to-year difference, and the repair stays as planned.

**1. Sample fidelity (proposal; build after the dashboards' scan, and after the repair).**

*Facts:*
- The PJM zones p122, p124 and p125 have no zeroed sites. Their sampled days give 0.96–0.99 of the full-record profile
  CF in 2028, but only 0.80–0.90 in 2030 and 2035.
- 17–20 of the 25 sampled days come from 2007–2011, the zeroed years.
- The selector (`s0_workflow/day_selection.py`) matches mean solar and wind CF per transreg and nationally, within
  absolute tolerances of 0.005 and 0.010, with every cluster counting equally. Nothing holds individual zones.
- Those targets and daily CFs are computed from profiles that still carry the zeroed years. So the scan should be
  rerun on repaired profiles before any fidelity fix is sized.

*Options:*
- **(A) Zone-level rows in the selector** (`time_sampling.cf_fidelity: zone_rows`). Add a row per zone and technology
  (solar, onshore wind, offshore wind) with eligible capacity above a floor: |sample-weighted mean − full-record mean|
  ≤ 3% of the full-record mean.
  - The rows are soft, penalised in the objective; the existing load and transreg rows stay hard.
  - If residuals stay above 3%, a swap step replaces the worst-contributing sample day with the record day that most
    reduces the largest relative error, then re-solves. The day count stays at 24 plus tails.
  - This keeps the load fit and the load–weather co-variation in the sample, but can't promise ±3% in every zone with
    24 days.
- **(B) CF rescaling of the sampled profiles** (`time_sampling.cf_fidelity: rescale`). For each variable cluster, the
  scale is (full-record mean CF) / (sample-weighted mean CF over the representative days; stress days excluded).
  - Apply it to the cluster's sampled profile, clip at 1, and push the clipped energy onto the unclipped hours (2–3
    passes).
  - Bounds: scales outside 0.8–1.25 are applied but warned.
  - It needs the full-record cluster means, which pg_to_switch has (`period_variability` before sampling), to be written
    out (`variable_cf_full_record.csv`).
  - It runs before the wind-loss step, so that factor applies once to both.
  - Report: `vcf_fidelity.csv`, with the scale per cluster and the clipped share.
  - Energy matches exactly per cluster. The cost is distorted hourly levels on the sampled days (peaks scale too), and it
    does nothing for load–weather co-variation.

*Recommendation:* (B) as the guaranteed backstop. It is deterministic and simple to test, and it closes whatever (A)
leaves. Add (A) if the scan shows large zone residuals concentrated where load also matters. Set both at ±3% against
the full record, on repaired profiles.

*Run-time and fit with the planned workflow (Tom, 11 Oct): option B, with the run time kept in check.*
- **Planned workflow:** (1) regional models through the region-split functionality (`region_scope`: a detail region at
  zone level, the rest aggregated) for regionally focused work; (2) large-sample production-cost iterations
  (`adjust/make_split_models.py`, builds reused from the expansion run) for reliability testing.
- **No added LP size.** B is a pre-processing step on `variable_capacity_factors.csv`: no new variables, constraints or
  days. Its cost is seconds in the case build. Fidelity is never bought with more sample days: `n_days` stays as set,
  and A, if ever used, swaps days instead of adding them.
- **Applies to the expansion sample only.** The production-cost runs sample many days or the full record, so they need
  no rescaling. The scale is about 1 there and should be left off: `cf_fidelity: off` on production-cost builds, or the
  step skips itself when the sample covers the record.
  - The split runs must start from a build without scaled profiles. Otherwise a split of the expansion case's inputs
    would inherit the scaling.
  - Each expansion case writes `vcf_fidelity.csv` (scale per cluster), so differences between the expansion and
    production-cost runs can be attributed.
- **Regional models:** B works per cluster as built, so aggregated zones (combined clusters) are rescaled the same way.
  The ±3% check is reported for the detail region's zones; aggregated zones only need regional fidelity.
  - If A is ever needed, it can be limited to the detail region's zones (a few dozen soft rows in the selector LP, which
    solves in seconds), and the Switch model is unchanged.
- **Reliability stays with the production-cost loop.** B corrects energy (mean CF), not tails. Low-wind or low-solar
  extremes and the stress days stay with the existing stress-day rules and the production-cost iteration's feedback.
  B leaves stress days (weight 0) untouched, so it doesn't weaken that loop.

**2. Time alignment (for the dashboards' load check).**
- The profiles are in UTC; the model's hours are UTC−5 (`model_definition.yml` `utc_offset: -5`). PowerGenome shifts
  the resource profiles.
- `regional_load_fn` loads are applied as-is, with no offset (`demand.yml`). `make_study_loads.py` (lines 115–121)
  rolls them from UTC to `utc_offset` when it writes the file, so load and profiles share UTC−5 if the file came from
  that script.
- `demand_response_fn` must already be in the model time zone (`flexible_load.yml`). Its builder isn't in this repo,
  so its basis needs checking.
- The PRM thermal derate converts temperatures to `temperature_tz: Etc/GMT+6` (UTC−6, ReEDS's convention;
  `s0_production.yml`, `prm.py`). If the model's hours are UTC−5, the hourly derates are one hour off. That is a small
  effect, but it should be aligned: `Etc/GMT+5`, or derived from `utc_offset`.

**3. p1 CF basis.**
- **The gap is p1-wide and uniform.** The 91 block-0 sites sit at 0.763 of their supply-curve CF (std 0.024). Their
  unaffected, most-correlated neighbours sit at 0.764 (std 0.012). So the gap has nothing to do with the zeroing.
- **A systematic factor, not geography or mapping.** A factor this uniform is what a different normalisation would
  give. A CPA→Site mapping error or local cloudiness would scatter it. It is close to 1/1.31.
- **Specific to p1.** Elsewhere the median is 0.95, p5 0.84, and PJM 0.86–0.89 on the full record. So a single
  national DC/AC convention can't be the cause.
- **The 5-day sample doesn't settle the normalisation.** In the committed fedpol case, most PV clusters peak at
  0.85–0.99 and p1 cluster 1 at 0.84, which doesn't point to a DC-normalised cap of about 0.77 in p1.

*Checks on the VM, in order:*
1. **Supply-curve columns:** list the columns of `solar_lcoe_ReEDS.csv` that carry basis information (capacity factor
   AC/DC, `dc_ac_ratio`/ILR, capacity AC/DC MW, tracking, tilt, losses). Compare p1, p5 and PJM rows against the
   median zone. A different ILR, a DC-basis CF or a different tracking setting on p1's rows settles it.
2. **Profile normalisation:** each site's 99.9th-percentile hourly CF over 2012–13, by zone. A cap near 1/ILR in p1
   only means DC-normalised profiles there; near 1.0 everywhere means the profiles agree and the supply-curve CF is the
   outlier.
3. **Supply-curve vintage:** check that p1's supply-curve rows and its profiles come from the same reV run (20240801).
   The patched resource groups (`ReEDS-cpas-patched`) may carry CFs from another run for some zones.

*If the supply-curve CF is the outlier,* the model's p1 profiles are right after the repair and only the 118-pair
comparison needs the basis noted. *If the profiles are DC-normalised in p1,* rescale p1's profiles by the ILR in the
repair folder (a second, flagged repair rule).

## 113. S0 v3.2: Storage Credit Phase Basis `span_mean` (Tom's Decision A)

**Date:** 2026-10-12. **Branch:** `tom/s0-v3.2`.

**The setting:** `s0_production.storage_credit.phase_basis: span_mean` is the v3.2 setting. The code default
(`production.STORAGE_CREDIT_DEFAULTS`) changes to match.
- **Effect:** with `mode: obbba`, each stage's new-build battery capex uses the mean OBBBA phase over its build years.
  The 2035 stage (2031–35) pays 1 − 0.30 × 0.85 = **0.745 × ATB** before the ×1.2 tariff. 2028 and 2030 pay 0.70;
  2040 and 2045 pay 1.00.
- **The flag itself is unchanged:** `mode: flat` (v3.1, ×0.7 in every period) stays the default until Tom approves v3.2,
  so builds are unchanged.

**Test:** `test_v32_setting_is_span_mean`. The regression-row resolution test lists the new value.

**Also:** the storage-credit comment in `s0_production.yml` had ended up above the PV-repair block; it is back above
its key.

## 114. S0 v3.2: CA Free-Tranche Presets, D2 as the v3.2 Value and D3 as a Sensitivity (Tom's Decision B)

**Date:** 2026-10-12. **Branch:** `tom/s0-v3.2`.

**The flag:** `s0_production.ca_wa_carbon.two_tranche.preset` (`production.CA_FREE_TRANCHE_PRESETS`). Every preset is
held flat in every period. A trajectory waits for CARB MRR data.

| Preset | Free tranche | Basis | Role |
|---|---|---|---|
| `v31` | 49.4 TWh (49,399 GWh) | CEC 2023 TSEG specified clean imports: large and small hydro, nuclear, wind, solar | **Default**: S0 v3.1, unchanged until v3.2 is approved |
| `d2` | 52.7 TWh (52,721 GWh) | v31 + geothermal 2,569 + biomass 753 GWh | **v3.2 value** (Tom) |
| `d3` | 39.0 TWh (38,972 GWh) | CARB basis, indicative: v31 less the extra unspecified volume CARB counts (about 20.8 TWh from the CARB 2022 inventory via Next10, an upper bound, less CEC's 10,373 GWh) | **Sensitivity**: replace with CARB MRR data when it can be read |

**Usage:**
- A sensitivity row sets `s0_production: {ca_wa_carbon: {two_tranche: {preset: d3}}}`.
- Setting both a preset and `free_mwh_per_yr` stops the build.
- The tranche info file records the preset's source.

**Test:** `test_ca_free_tranche_presets`.
- The yml default is `v31`, and the explicit `v31` setting writes byte-identical tranche files.
- `d2` and `d3` write 52.721 and 38.972 TWh in every period.
- Unknown presets, and a preset combined with `free_mwh_per_yr`, are refused.
- The regression-row resolution test lists the new key.

## 116. S0 v3.2: Sample-Fidelity Rescale, Option B (Tom's Decision D)

**Date:** 2026-10-12. **Branch:** `tom/s0-v3.2`. **Flag:** `s0_production.time_sampling.cf_fidelity.mode`: `off` (default,
v3.1) or `rescale`.

**The scan** (`data/rev-cf-mismatch` dbc3637, copied to `s0_workflow/data/sample_fidelity/`). S0 v3.1, sampled vs
full-record CF, zone-stages outside 0.95–1.05:

| Series | Zone-stages outside | Share of MW | Typical deviation | Range |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Load | 1 of 670 | – | – | – |
| PV | 110 of 670 | 16% | 2.7% | – |
| Onshore wind | 422 of 660 | 63% | 7% | 0.66–1.28 |
| Offshore wind | 127 of 180 | – | 10% | 0.64–1.40 |

National ratios are 0.95–1.01, because the zone errors cancel. The scan also confirms that load and profiles share the
UTC−5 basis (`tz_check_by_zone.csv`).

**Rescale** (`s0_workflow/cf_fidelity.py`, called in `pg_to_switch.operational_files` before
`variable_capacity_factors.csv`):
- **Scale:** for every PV, onshore and offshore wind cluster, scale = full-record mean CF / sample-weighted mean CF,
  with weights ts_scale_to_period × ts_duration_of_tp.
- **Cap:** the sampled profile is multiplied by the scale and capped at 1. The capped energy is put back on the
  uncapped hours (up to 5 proportional passes), so the sample-weighted CF equals the full-record CF; any residual is
  reported.
- **Left alone:** stress days (weight 0), distributed PV, hydro and every other technology.
- **Flag:** scales outside 0.8–1.25 are applied and flagged in the log and in `vcf_fidelity.csv` (per cluster and model
  year: CFs before and after, scale, capped share, residual, flag).
- **Pre-processing only:** the same days, timepoints and model size.

**Option A is dropped for national runs, and the national sample size does not grow** (Tom: balance fidelity against
run time). Fidelity beyond B comes from regional split models (more days in a smaller model) and large-sample
production-cost runs, which must start from a build without B. These are design notes in `s0_v3.2.md`.

**Order:** turn it on after the PV repair (§111). Rerun the scan on repaired profiles first; the runbook,
`scripts/README_pv_repair.md` (§117), has the steps.

**Test:** `test_cf_fidelity.py`.
- Weights, with stress days at 0.
- The rescaled sample-weighted CF equals the full-record CF for PV, onshore and offshore wind.
- Stress days and other technologies are untouched; the rows and columns are unchanged.
- Capping redistributes energy, and big scales are flagged.
- A cluster with no output on the sampled days is left as it is and flagged.
- The default is off, and the wiring sits before the capacity-factor table.

## 118. S0 v3.2: PRM Temperature Derates on the Model's Clock (Item F)

**Date:** 2026-10-12. **Branch:** `tom/s0-v3.2`. **Flag:** `s0_production.prm.thermal_derate.prm_temp_tz_fix`: `false`
(default, v3.1) or `true`.

**The problem:**
- The hourly thermal derate read ReEDS temperatures in `temperature_tz: Etc/GMT+6` (UTC−6) and matched them to the
  stress day's model hours.
- The model's hours are UTC−5: `utc_offset: -5`. The profiles and load are on that basis (dbc3637 time-zone check).
- So each model hour took the temperature of the hour before it.

**The fix:** with the flag on, temperatures are read on the model's own clock (`prm.model_tz`: `utc_offset` −5 →
`Etc/GMT+5`). The code is `prm.temperature_tz` and `prm.hour_temperature`.

**Effect on S0:** none as configured. S0 uses `method: seasonal`, which doesn't read temperatures, so the flag acts only
with `method: hourly`.

**Test:** `test_prm_temp_tz_fix_peak_hour_matches_model_hour`. Temperatures stored in UTC peak at 21:00 UTC, which is
16:00 model time.
- With the fix, the hottest hour and the largest derate both fall at model hour 16.
- With v3.1 they fall at 15.
- The yml default is `false`, and the regression-row and S-set tests list the key.

## 119. S0 v3.2: Start-Up Costs of New Gas, V32-01 (Item G). Changes Results; for Ollie's Review

**Date:** 2026-10-12. **Branch:** `tom/s0-v3.2`. **Flag:** `s0_production.startup_cost_fix.enabled`: `false` (default,
v3.1) or `true`. It is a separate flag, so its effect on S0 can be shown on its own.

**The bug:**
- PowerGenome matches `startup_costs.yml` `new_build_startup_costs` keys to new builds with `str.contains`
  (`generators.py:296`, as the housekeeping review found).
- The keys `NaturalGas_CT` and `NaturalGas_CC` match the older ATB names (`NaturalGas_CTAvgCF`, `_CCAvgCF`), not those
  S0 builds: `NaturalGas_Combustion Turbine (F-Frame)` and `NaturalGas_1-on-1 Combined Cycle (H-Frame)`.
- **Confirmed in the committed s4x1 fedpol inputs:** all 268 new CT and CC projects (134 zones × 2) have
  `gen_startup_om` 0, while existing CTs have $145/MW-start and existing CCs $112.

**The fix** (`s0_workflow/startup_costs.py`, applied to the case's settings by `production.apply_settings`). It adds keys
without regex metacharacters (`str.contains` reads keys as regular expressions) and keeps the v3.1 keys for the older
names:

| Key | Class |
|---|---|
| `NaturalGas_Combustion Turbine` | `gas_large_ct` |
| `NaturalGas_1-on-1 Combined Cycle` (also its 95% and 97% CCS variants) | `gas_cc` |
| `NaturalGas_2-on-1 Combined Cycle` | `gas_cc` |
| `Coal_95%-CCS`, `Coal_99%-CCS` | `coal_supercritical` |

The coal CCS keys cover technologies the CCS axes build; v3.1 gave them no start cost. S0 builds no coal, so on S0 the
fix changes gas only.

**Audit** (`audit`, over every technology name in `resources.yml` (`atb_new_gen`, `cost_multiplier_technology_map`)
and the `atb_new_gen` lists of the scenario axes):
- **v3.1:**
  - The current ATB CT and CC names, the CC CCS variants and `Coal_95%/99%-CCS` match no key, so they get no start-up
    cost.
  - `NaturalGas_CT/CC`, `Coal_new`, `Coal_CCS30` and `Nuclear_Nuclear` match as intended.
  - `Coal_CCS90`, `Coal_IGCC` and `NaturalGas_CCS100` match nothing (stale, harmless).
- **With the fix:** every NaturalGas_* name and every fossil or nuclear technology an axis or `atb_new_gen` builds
  matches exactly one key. There are no ambiguous matches, and no hydrogen technology exists.

**Build check:** after the case is written, any new `NaturalGas_*` project with `gen_startup_om` 0 stops the build
when the flag is on. With the flag off it is logged, which is the v3.1 behaviour.

**Test:** `test_startup_costs.py`.
- The v3.1 keys miss the gas names, and the fixed keys map them.
- Every buildable fossil and nuclear name matches exactly one key.
- The flag is off by default, and turning it on adds the keys.
- The build check flags all 268 fedpol projects with the flag off, stops the build with it on, and passes once
  `gen_startup_om` is set.
- The regression-row and S-set tests list the key.

**Results:** with the flag on, new CTs and CCs pay start-up costs in commitment (`commit_operate`), which favours
existing units and changes dispatch and builds. **For Ollie's review.**

## 120. S0 v3.2: Offshore Wind Duplicates (Item H)

**Date:** 2026-10-12. **Branch:** `tom/s0-v3.2`. **Flag:** `s0_production.offshore_filter_fix.mode`: `off` (default,
v3.1) or `split_groups`.

**The finding** (dashboards, dbc3637; confirmed in the committed s4x1 fedpol inputs):
- In all 36 offshore zones, the three offshore entries in `resources.yml` (fixed pref-site 1, fixed pref-site 0,
  floating) produce the same project: every eligible CPA, floating included.
- Each carries the zone's whole capacity limit: 2,985,509 MW each for `OffShoreWind_Class3_Moderate_fixed_0`, `_fixed_1`
  and `OffShoreWind_Class12_Moderate_floating_0` (p1: 68,339 MW each). The resource is offered three times, not twice.

**Cause:**
- PowerGenome selects a resource group by the cluster entry's keys against the group JSON's attributes (`technology`,
  `metadata`, `profiles`, ...; the format `make_study_loads.py` writes).
- The offshore group in `ReEDS-cpas-patched` has no `turbine_type` or `pref_site` attribute, so all three entries select
  the one all-CPA group.

**The fix** (`s0_workflow/offshore_groups.py`; `s0_workflow/scripts/split_offshore_groups.py`):
- **Split:** writes a copy of `RESOURCE_GROUPS` with the offshore group split into one group per (turbine type, pref
  site). Each group's JSON is the original plus `turbine_type` and `pref_site`; each gets its metadata subset. The
  profiles and site map are shared, and every other file is hard-linked.
- **Classification:** turbine type comes from a metadata column, or from water depth (floating deeper than 60 m by
  default). The pref site comes from a metadata column. Column names are arguments, matched without regard to case; a
  missing one stops the run with the list of columns.
- **Manifest:** `offshore_true_resource_by_zone.csv` (MW at LCOE ≤ 125, the clusters' filter, by zone, turbine type and
  pref site) and `offshore_split_manifest.json`.

**The build:**
- With `split_groups` (`production.apply_offshore_filter_fix`), it checks the manifest and points `RESOURCE_GROUPS` at
  the copy.
- `write_offshore_check` writes `offshore_mw_by_zone.csv` in each case: the capacity limits by zone and technology,
  plus, with the fix, the true resource and the ratio. The build stops if any zone's limits don't sum to the true
  resource.
- With the flag off, it reports the v3.1 multiple: the zone total over the largest single project, 3.00.
- Before/after by zone: compare the off build's `offshore_mw_by_zone.csv` (each technology carrying the whole resource)
  with the on build's (summing to it once).
- Refused mismatches: with the flag off, a split folder in `RESOURCE_GROUPS`.

**Not known here (VM data):**
- The offshore group JSON's name and the metadata's turbine-type, depth and pref-site columns, which the split needs as
  arguments. Find the JSON with `grep -l -i offshorewind pg_data/resource_groups/**/*.json`.
- Whether PowerGenome's cluster cache needs clearing for the new groups.

**Test:** `test_offshore_groups.py`.
- The fedpol inputs show the ×3 duplication: identical limits in every zone, 36 zones, 2,985,509 MW.
- On a hand-built group folder, the split gives fixed/1, fixed/0 and floating/0 groups with the right CPAs and MW,
  shared profiles, the all-CPA group removed, other groups unchanged, the source untouched and the true resource by
  zone at the LCOE limit.
- The flag is off by default, the off-with-split folder is refused, and `split_groups` points `RESOURCE_GROUPS`.
- The case check passes at ×1 and stops at ×2.
- The CLI runs and reports a missing column.
- The regression-row and S-set tests list the key.

## 121. S0 v3.2 Build Rate R1: Ramp Base Includes the Previous Stage's Data Rate

**Date:** 2026-10-12. **Branch:** `tom/s0-v3.2`. **Flag:** `build_rate.ramp_base` (`pg/settings/build_rate.yml`):
`build` (default, v3.1) or `max_build_r`. Tom, 11 Oct 2026: the build-rate package R1 / R11 / R2 / R4 / R7 is in scope
for v3.2 and is the critical path; R3 (post-2030 growth) and R5 (regional floors) are v4. Shared code: Ollie reviews at
v3.2 sign-off (SHARED_CHANGES rows 123–127).

**v3.1:** the ramp bound on a stage's R is `(1+growth)^W × b + floor`, where `b` is the best build rate chained from
earlier stages (`build_rate_prev_build.csv`). A stage that built little locks the next stage's R near the
1,000 MW/yr floor, whatever the data path says.

**R1:** the base is `max(b, R_prev)`, where `R_prev` is the previous stage's data rate (`br_rate_data_mw` of its
committed period, chained as `br_prev_r_data_mw_per_yr`). R is then never ramp-limited below `(1+growth)^W × R_prev +
floor`.
- **Within one stage** (mode B windows, perfect foresight): `max(build_q, R_q)` is not linear. The ramp between two
  periods of one stage is dropped when `(1+growth)^W × R_q + floor` already reaches R's upper bound. Otherwise it keeps
  the v3.1 form. S0 production is mode A, one period per stage, so this case does not arise there.
- **Code:**
  - `switch/study_modules/build_rate.py`: `br_ramp_base`, read from `build_rate_options.csv`, and `ramp_rule`.
  - `switch/study_modules/prepare_next_stage.py::chain_build_rate_inputs` writes the two new columns, only when an
    option is on.
  - `build_rate/brc/switch_case.py` writes `build_rate_options.csv`, only when R1 or R11 is on.

**Before/after** (`s0_workflow/data/build_rate_v32/report_{central,high}.csv`; `build_rate/scripts/v32_report.py`):
- **Previous stage built nothing:**
  - v3.1: the ramp bound is the floor, 1.0 GW/yr in every stage and group, so it binds.
  - R1 (central): wind 10.1 / 11.5 / 13.2 / 16.6 GW/yr for 2030 / 35 / 40 / 45, solar 41.9 / 35.3 / 40.8 / 51.8 and
    storage 28.7 / 18.0 / 22.5 / 32.6. These are above the data R in every stage, so the data rate binds instead.
- **Previous stage built at or above R_prev:** R1 changes nothing.

**Test:** `build_rate/tests/test_build_rate.py`.
- `test_toy_r1_ramp_base_includes_previous_data_rate` (3-zone toy, HiGHS): chained build 0.2 and previous data rate 5.
  v3.1 holds R(2020) to 1.05¹⁰ × 0.2 and ignores the new column. R1 lets R exceed that, staying under the data rate.
- `test_chain_writes_v32_columns_only_with_options`.

## 122. S0 v3.2 Build Rate R11: R Floored at the Previous Stage's Build / 1.3

**Flag:** `build_rate.stage_r`: `data` (default, v3.1) or `max_data_prev_build`. The divisor is
`build_rate.prev_build_divisor`, default 1.3.

**R11:** in every period of a stage, `R ≤ max(br_rate_data_mw, b_prev / 1.3)`.
- `b_prev` is the previous stage's own build per year in its committed period, chained as
  `br_prev_stage_build_mw_per_yr`. It is that stage's build, not the best so far.
- Dividing by 1.3 puts the previous stage's build in the free band (edge 1.3 × R). The bands scale with R, so the
  2.0 × R ceiling is kept.
- **Code:**
  - The module's `br_rate_cap_mw` replaces `br_rate_data_mw` in `BR_Rate_Data`.
  - With R11 on, `build_rate_new_build.csv` gains `rate_cap_mw_per_yr`. It is unchanged when R11 is off.

**Before/after (central):** R11 binds only where the previous stage built more than 1.3 × this stage's data R. That is
the 2028 → 2030 hand-over.
- **Previous stage at its R (37.1 GW/yr solar, 23.7 storage):** 2030 R's bound rises from 26.9 to 28.5 GW/yr (solar)
  and from 11.6 to 18.3 (storage).
- **At its free band (1.3 R):** 2030 R's bound is 37.1 (solar) and 23.7 (storage), 2028's own R.
- **2035 onwards:** no change. The grown R is above `b_prev / 1.3` unless the previous stage built above 1.3 × R,
  which the free band allows only at a 15% adder.
- **Wind:** no change in any stage.

**Test:** `test_toy_r11_rate_floor_from_previous_stage_build`. With data rate 0.5 and previous build 2.6, the rate cap
is 2.0 and R exceeds 0.5. Solar, with nothing chained, keeps its data rate.

## 123. S0 v3.2 Build Rate R2: Smoothed 2028 → 2030 Hand-Over for Solar and Storage

**Flag:** `build_rate/config.yaml` `near_term.handover.mode`: `off` (default, v3.1) or `linear`. Groups `[solar,
storage]`, blend years `[2029, 2030]`. The case setting `build_rate.handover` must match the tables (see "Tables" below).

**v3.1:** R is the queue-based rate up to 2028 and R0 in 2029–30. Stage-window means: solar 37.1 → 26.9 GW/yr (−28%),
storage 23.7 → 11.6 (−51%).

**R2:** each blend year takes `max(v3.1 R, the straight line from R(2028) to R(2031))` (`rates.handover`).
- The grown path after 2030 is unchanged: it still grows from the v3.1 R(2030). Changing it is R3, which is v4.
- IPM-shaped levels (`high_ipm`) are not affected. Derived levels (`reform_bp`, `high_reform`) inherit the change
  through their base levels.

**Before/after, 2030 stage R (GW/yr):**

| Level | Solar | Storage |
|---|---|---|
| central | 26.9 → 30.8 (drop from 2028: −17% instead of −28%) | 11.6 → 16.6 (−30% instead of −51%) |
| high | 31.2 → 33.8 | 16.4 → 19.8 |

- Wind and the other stages are unchanged.
- The 2035 stage R stays at v3.1's (central solar 31.2, storage 14.6). So central storage still dips below its 2028
  level until 2040; that is R3.

**Test:** `test_r2_handover_linear_blend`: the blend values, the end points unchanged, unlisted groups and `off` give
v3.1, and the blend never lowers R.

## 124. S0 v3.2 Build Rate R4: Ramp Floor from ReEDS's Per-State Minimum Growth Bin

**Flag:** `build_rate.ramp_floor`: `placeholder` (default, v3.1: `ramp_floor_mw`, 1,000 MW/yr per group) or
`reeds_gbin_min`.

**Source:** ReEDS `inputs/growth_constraints/gbin_min.csv` at commit 2f583ff5 (`build_rate/data/reference/REEDS_COMMIT.txt`).
- Values: wind-ons 100, pv 100, battery 100 and gas 300 MW.
- `b_inputs.gms`: "gbin_min is based on the representative plant size for a single plant in that tech group".
  `d_solveoneyear.gms` raises each state's first, zero-cost growth bin to it in every modelled state.
- **Summed nationally:** × 48, the US states in `hierarchy.csv`. That gives 4,800 MW/yr for wind, solar and storage,
  and 14,400 for gas (gas is not in S0's groups).
- **Two differences from ReEDS, both noted in `config.yaml`:**
  - ReEDS floors the free bin (a max). Here the value is the additive floor in the ramp bound.
  - ReEDS applies it per state, not as one national number.

**Before/after:** the ramp bound rises by 3.8 GW/yr in every stage and group. It matters only where the ramp binds:
after a stage that built little, and with R1 off.

**Test:** `test_case_writer_v32_flags`: floor 1,000 by default and 4,800 with the flag. The v3.1 case writes no options
file.

## 125. S0 v3.2 Build Rate R7: Construction-Stage Completion from LBNL Queued Up

**Flag:** `build_rate/config.yaml` `near_term.construction_completion_source`: `placeholder` (default, v3.1: 0.9) or
`lbnl_post_ia`. The case setting `build_rate.construction_completion` must match the tables.

**Source:** Rand, Cheyette, Gorman, Wiser, Seel, Kahrl (LBNL) with Talley and Zhang (Interconnection.fyi), *Queued Up:
Interconnection Queue Dataset & Summarized Data Files, through 2025*, May 2026 (emp.lbl.gov/queues). This is the
`queued_up.xlsx` the pipeline already reads.
- Sheet "27. Post-IA Completion", table (B) "Status by Years Since IA Execution". It is compiled from LBNL's annual
  datasets: 7 ISOs and 7 non-ISO balancing areas with IA dates.
- **Rate:** in the latest bucket (4–5 years after IA), operational / (operational + withdrawn + suspended) =
  629 / 871 = **0.722**. Count basis, all technologies.
- **Earlier buckets:** 0.535 (1–2 years), 0.656 (2–3) and 0.722 (3–4).
- **Same sheet, IAs signed 2000–2022, capacity basis:** 43% operational, 41% withdrawn, 15% active, 1% suspended.
- **Why this bucket:** LBNL publishes no completion rate for the construction stage, and Queued Up has no resolved
  construction history (completed projects are reclassified as IA Executed: 194 active and 4 withdrawn construction
  requests). Under-construction requests are the furthest-along IA-executed ones, so the latest bucket is the closest
  LBNL figure.
- **Choosing another bucket:** `near_term.lbnl_post_ia_bucket`.
- **Code:**
  - `brc/data.py::load_post_ia_completion` reads the table from the file.
  - `rates.completion_rates` uses the rate and records the source string in `completion_rates.csv`.

**Effect:** the construction stage is a small share of the near-term queue. Active MW with an executed IA vs under
construction: solar 197.7 / 20.2 GW, storage 119.0 / 1.4, wind 55.0 / 3.2. So 0.9 → 0.722 lowers the queue years
slightly:

| 2028 stage R (GW/yr) | v3.1 | R7 |
|---|---|---|
| Solar | 37.1 | 36.3 (−2.2%) |
| Storage | 23.74 | 23.69 |
| Wind | 8.28 | 8.25 (R0 binds) |

- With R2 on, central solar 2030 is 30.4 instead of 30.8.
- No other stage changes, because R0 binds from 2029.

**Test:** `test_r7_lbnl_post_ia_completion` (fixture of the published table; v3.1 gives 0.9; a missing table errors)
and `test_r7_reads_lbnl_sheet_when_available` (the real file).

### Tables, flags, the pin test and the expected effect (§121–§125 together)

**Tables (R2, R7):**
- `python -m brc.cli run --outputs outputs_v32 --set near_term.handover.mode=linear --set
  near_term.construction_completion_source=lbnl_post_ia` writes a separate table set. Its `run_info.yaml` records
  `flags`.
- Point `build_rate.tables_dir: build_rate/outputs_v32` at it.
- The case build stops if `build_rate.handover` or `build_rate.construction_completion` differs from the tables' flags,
  in either direction. Missing flags count as v3.1.
- With every flag off, the pipeline's tables are byte-identical to v3.1's. Checked here on the same EIA-860M (August
  2026) and Queued Up files: every CSV is identical.

**Pin test (`s0_workflow/tests/test_build_rate_v3_pinned.py`), updated deliberately:**
- `build_rate_v3_manifest.csv` stays as the record of v3 (79f4064), still checked against git.
- The new `build_rate_v32_manifest.csv` pins the current content.
- `test_v32_changes_only_the_listed_entries` lists the entries allowed to differ from v3: `brc/cli.py`, `brc/data.py`,
  `brc/rates.py`, `brc/switch_case.py`, `config.yaml`, `pg/settings/build_rate.yml` and the module's model.
- `write_build_rate_files` in `pg_to_switch.py` is unchanged.

**Before/after tables:** `s0_workflow/data/build_rate_v32/`.
- `stage_r.csv`: R by level, group and stage for v3.1, R2, R7 and both.
- `report_{central,high}.csv`: the ramp bound under v3.1, R1, R4 and R1+R4, and R11's cap, for a previous-stage build
  of 0, 0.5, 1, 1.3 and 2 × R_prev.
- `completion_rates_r7.csv`.
- Committed (predetermined) builds, which raise R and the ramp, are left out. The S0 cases' build history is on the
  VM; these tables use the data R and stylised previous builds.

**Expected effect on S1 / S2-type cases:**
- **S1** (central, credits reinstated) builds more wind, solar and storage than S0, so the build rate binds more often.
  - R2 and R11 loosen the 2030 stage, the one where v3.1's R drops: solar +3.9 GW/yr of R and storage +5.1 (R2). That
    raises the 2029–30 ceiling (2 × R × 2 years) by up to 15.7 GW for solar and 20.3 GW for storage, and by more with
    R11 if 2028 built near its ceiling.
  - R1 and R4 matter only after an underbuilt stage. In S0-type chains that is wind in 2028 if little was built.
  - R7 trims 2028 solar R by 0.8 GW/yr.
- **S2 / S4** (high): the same direction, smaller in relative terms (2030 solar +2.7, storage +3.4 GW/yr with R2). The
  high growth rates already keep the ramp clear of R.
- **L, P (high_reform), S3, S5 (off):** L and P change through the same channels. S3 and S5 have no build rate, so
  they are unaffected.
- **Results:** R1, R11 and R2 only relax limits, so in any stage where those limits bind they can only add wind, solar
  and storage (and lower cost), and nowhere else. R7 tightens 2028 slightly. The measured effect needs a solved
  one-stage test: see `Guides and documentation/s0_v3.2.md`.

## 126. S0 v3.2 Defaults ON (Tom's Decision, 11 Oct 2026)

**Date:** 2026-10-12. **Branch:** `tom/s0-v3.2`. These change S0's inputs and results. Every S0 production row (S0prod_A
/ B, BILL, the S-set, `s4x1_S0prod_2035_new` and the sensitivities) takes them. Cases with S0 off are unaffected.

| Item | Setting | v3.1 | CHANGES |
|---|---|---|---|
| a. Wind interconnection price | `settings.interconnection_headroom.price_factors: {wind: 1.0}` | `{wind: null}` (0.25 ×) | §98 |
| b / A. Battery storage credit | `storage_credit.mode: obbba`, `phase_basis: span_mean` | `flat` (×0.7 every period) | §100, §113 |
| B. CA free tranche | `ca_wa_carbon.two_tranche.preset: d2` (52.7 TWh/yr) | `v31` (49.4) | §114 |
| D. Sample-fidelity rescale | `time_sampling.cf_fidelity.mode: rescale` | `off` | §116 |
| G. New-gas start-up costs (V32-01) | `startup_cost_fix.enabled: true` | `false` | §119 |
| F. PRM temperature clock | `prm.thermal_derate.prm_temp_tz_fix: true` | `false` (inert with S0's seasonal derates) | §118 |

**Not yet ON:**

  checks), then `profiles_dir` set.
- `offshore_filter_fix: split_groups` (§120). Needs the split folder from the VM (group JSON name, turbine-type / depth
  and pref-site columns), then `resource_groups_dir` set.

  build rescales the unrepaired PV profiles. The full-record target then includes the zeroed years, so PV clusters with
  zeroed site-years are scaled towards the depressed CF. Turn on the repair before the v3.2 S0 build.

**The regression row (`s4x1_S0prod_2035`, axis value `on_pgdays`) stays the v3.1 build.**
- `on_pgdays` sets each of the six back to its v3.1 value: wind `null`, `storage_credit.mode: flat`, preset `v31`,
  `cf_fidelity.mode: off`, `startup_cost_fix.enabled: false` and `prm_temp_tz_fix: false`.
- `test_regression_row_resolves_as_before_the_one_column_fix` lists the resolved differences from 9e2045b. All are v3.1
  values: the flags at v3.1, plus the explicit wind `null`.
- `on_pgdays_new` (`s4x1_S0prod_2035_new`, "the same case with the new defaults") takes v3.2.

**Tests updated deliberately:**

  - `test_ca_wa_carbon.py::test_ca_free_tranche_presets`;

  - `test_prm.py` (two tests);
  - `test_startup_costs.py`;
  - `test_s0_production.py`: the headroom block, and the battery modifier ×1.0 under obbba;
  - `test_final_s0.py`: preset `d2`.
- `test_s_set_on_v32.py`: the price-factor paths join `V32_FLAGS`. The byte-for-byte check of
  `scenario_management.yml` against the credits branch (f8f12bc) becomes

## 127. S0 v3.2: WA Import Charge Reassessed (Item I)

**Date:** 2026-10-12. **Branch:** `tom/s0-v3.2`. No setting changes. `wa_import_charge` stays `per_mwh` (v3.1). The
`two_tranche` code (§100) is kept with no value: free 0 is the placeholder that reproduces v3.1.

**The runner's figure:** S0 v3.1 2028 WA charged border imports = 0.367 TWh/yr (367,286 MWh).
- This is `delivered_mwh_per_yr` from `trans_import_cost_results.csv`, summed over p5→p2, p14→p2, p5→p3, p14→p3 and
  p17→p3. It is delivered after losses and gross, with no export netting.
- At $21.24/MWh this is about $7.8M/yr.
- The Canadian import generators in p1 / p3 are not in this set: they are charged as generators, at factor 0.
- **So §100's candidate is withdrawn.** It was free = 2028 imports − 13.2 TWh, where 13.2 TWh is Commerce's CY2023
  retail "unspecified" fuel-mix volume. That is a retail quantity, not the model's basis, and the formula is negative.

**Data-based proposal (value pending):**
- **Formula:** free tranche = s_clean × the model's 2028 charged imports (0.367 TWh), held flat.
  - s_clean = specified imports with emission factor about 0 / all imports.
  - Both counted from sources outside WA and CA, Canadian sources excluded (the model carries those as import
    generators). EIM purchases count as unspecified.
  - From Ecology's EPE reports (WAC 173-441-124: specified MWh by facility and first point of receipt, unspecified
    MWh, EIM MWh), latest reporting year.
- **Not reached:** ecology.wa.gov, apps.ecology.wa.gov and data.wa.gov fail DNS from this environment (network
  policy).
- **What Tom needs to download:**
  1. Ecology's GHG Reporting Program publication data, latest year (released each January): the "Mandatory
     greenhouse gas reports" page, or the data.wa.gov dataset "GHG Reporting Program Publication", rows for electric
     power entities.
  2. If that gives only emissions by entity, not MWh by source, ask Ecology's GHG reporting program (CCA questions:
     CCAQuestions@ecy.wa.gov, 360-407-6963) for an aggregate of EPE imported MWh by source type for the latest year:
     - specified by fuel or emission factor and by first point of receipt;
     - unspecified;
     - EIM.
  3. Optional: Ecology publication 23-02-090 (Interim Guidance for EPE Reporting), for the reporting definitions.
- **Upper bound on the effect:** s_clean × $7.8M/yr in 2028.

**Does the charge matter?** At 0.37 TWh and $7.8M/yr in 2028, almost certainly not for system results. WA's
1.3 → 2.9 Mt rise (2028 → 2045) is more likely driven by something else.
- **Candidates:**
  1. WA gas serving CA. CA↔WA flows pay no import charge, CA's free tranche bound in every S0 v3.1 stage, and a WA
     combined cycle (~0.37 t/MWh at the linked price) is a cheaper route into CA than a charged import
     (0.428 t/MWh × price).
  2. WA load growth outpacing WA's clean supply, with hydro fixed.
  3. Charged WA imports growing in later stages. Unknown: the figure above is 2028 only.
- **Tools:**
  - `s0_workflow/scripts/wa_emissions_drivers.py` reads the solved S0 v3.1 stages and writes, per stage:
    - WA generation and emissions by fuel;
    - flows on every WA line (to and from CA and other states, with the charge);
    - WA charged imports;
    - the CA tranche;
    - the change from 2028 by fuel.
  - **Fixture test:** `test_wa_emissions_drivers.py`.
- **One-stage test (recipe K):**
  - S0prod_A 2028 from the tag s0-v3.1 with one uncommitted edit, `ca_wa_carbon.import_tco2_per_mwh: {CA: 0.428,
    WA: 0.0}`, so WA border imports cost 0. CA and WA's own emissions are unchanged.
  - Compare with the S0 v3.1 2028 stage.
  - If the charge is immaterial, costs move by ≤ about $8M/yr and WA emissions by noise.
  - This is cleaner than a two_tranche run because it needs no new code path or row.

## 128. S0 v3.2: PowerGenome Submodule Pin 5ecb454 → c4c433c (V32-04)

**Date:** 2026-10-12. **Branch:** `tom/s0-v3.2`. From the repo manager's PowerGenome dependency inventory (V32-04).
S0-block housekeeping, alongside §115 (the S-set files brought onto v3.2). No result change expected.

**The problem:**
- `pg/settings/resources.yml` sets `cost_multiplier_fn: AEO_2026_regional_cost_corrections.csv`, S0's regional capex
  table.
- That file exists only from PowerGenome c4c433c on, but the repo pinned the submodule at 5ecb454.
- So a fresh clone followed by `git submodule update` couldn't build S0's regional capex. The runner's checkout
  already had the file.

**Choice: bump the pin (preferred) rather than move the CSV into `pg/extra_inputs`.** Checked in a scratch clone of
`TMBrindle/PowerGenome`, not the repo's submodule checkout:
- **Exact match:** c4c433c ("Add AEO2026 regional cost correction table") sits directly on top of 5ecb454. The diff is
  one file, `data/cost_multipliers/AEO_2026_regional_cost_corrections.csv` (+26 lines). The pinned code is unchanged.
- **Reachable:** c4c433c is on the remote's `master`; 5ecb454 is `edf-modifications`. A fresh clone fetches it.
- **No PowerGenome change needed:** PowerGenome reads `cost_multiplier_fn` from its own `data/cost_multipliers/`.
  Moving the CSV would need a PowerGenome path change or a second copy of the table, and I didn't read PowerGenome's
  code to check how it resolves the name.
- **EDF-org migration:** the migrated fork has to carry c4c433c, a single commit on top of what it carries anyway.
  Fast-forwarding `edf-modifications` to c4c433c in the fork would put the pin back on the EDF branch. That is outside
  this repo's scope, so it isn't done here.

**Change:**
- The `PowerGenome` gitlink is now c4c433cfefd0ae11f5b3f47ae05e3b9d7c90a691. `.gitmodules` is unchanged.
- The local submodule checkout was not touched.
- **Test:** `s0_workflow/tests/test_powergenome_pin.py` checks the gitlink, plus the file's presence where the
  submodule is checked out at the pin; it is skipped otherwise.

**Effect:** S0 inputs should be byte-identical to a build from a checkout that already had the file (the runner's).
The runner's v3.2 test build will confirm it.

## 131. S0 v3.2: PV Repair Without Coordinates, Neighbours by Daily Correlation

**Date:** 2026-10-12. **Branch:** `tom/s0-v3.2`. Fixes the VM block on runbook E (§117).

**The block:** `repair_pv_profiles.py` stopped at "lat: no column 'latitude'". No coordinate source exists on the VM:
- none of the 11 copies of `solar_lcoe_ReEDS.csv` has latitude/longitude;
- neither does `solar_site_mapping_20240801.parquet` (CPA_ID, Site, profile_dist);
- nor does the profile file.

**Chosen: (b), a coordinate-free neighbour method (`--neighbour-method correlation`), not (a), a coordinate table.**
- **(a) has no source in hand.** It would need reV supply-curve sc_point files (a download) and a CPA →
  `sc_point_gid` key that can't be verified from here; a wrong key would fill from the wrong sites silently.
  Coordinates were also only ever a proxy for what the fill needs: shared weather in the same hours.
- **(b) uses only files the VM has** and measures shared weather directly, in the years where the zeroed sites have
  data (2012–13).
- **(b) has a precedent:** the dashboards chose p1 block-0 neighbours the same way (`p1_block0_vs_neighbours.csv`,
  fd4a3a1). Their profile correlations were 0.83–0.86, and their 2012–13 levels were within 2% (median ratio 0.98).

**The method (`pv_profile_repair.plan_correlation`):**
- **Neighbour:** for each zeroed site-year, the site in the same zone whose daily output over 2012–13 (730 days)
  correlates best (Pearson) with the zeroed site's own, among sites with data in that weather year and in both
  reference years.
- **Fallback:** if the zone has no such site, the best-correlated site anywhere (status `filled outside zone`, counted
  in the manifest).
- **Scale and clip:** unchanged. The 2012–13 mean ratio, clipped to [0, 1].
- **Why daily sums:** the diurnal shape is common to every site, so hourly correlation is inflated by it; the day-to-day
  variation carries the shared clouds. 730 × 68,923 values also fit in memory, where the 17,520 hourly rows would need
  4.8 GB.
- **Implementation:** `scan` collects the daily sums in the same pass.
- **Sites table** (`repair_pv_profiles.sites_zone_from`): site, zone, capacity_mw.
  - Zone = the zone holding most of the site's CPA MW. `zones` counts the zones a site spans.
  - Built from the CPA metadata (`--region-col`, default `ipm_region`) and the site mapping.

**Manifest and log:**
- The manifest records `neighbour_method` and `neighbour_basis`, plus correlation quantiles and site-years filled
  outside their zone in `fill_summary`.
- The log has `correlation` and `scope` columns.
- `distance` remains the default method and is unchanged.

**Runbook (`README_pv_repair.md`):**
- Uses the correlation method, with output to the dashboards' scratch folder, `--hash` and `--no-links` (copies).
- Pass checks for the correlation fields: `correlation_p10` ≥ 0.8, review below 0.6, any fill outside the zone
  reviewed.

**Tests (`test_pv_profile_repair.py`):**
- The same-zone, same-weather neighbour is chosen over a better-correlated site in another zone.
- The daily sums equal the hourly data summed by day.
- Fills outside the zone.
- Errors for a missing zone column and an unknown method.
- `sites_zone_from` (majority-MW zone).
- The CLI end to end with `--hash --no-links`.

## 132. S0 v3.2 Defaults: PV Profile Repair ON (Tom's Conditional Approval; the VM Run Passed)

**Date:** 2026-10-12. **Branch:** `tom/s0-v3.2`.

**The run** (dashboards session, runbook dc7aa00, correlation neighbours):
- **Fills:** all 14,350 zeroed site-years filled, all in-zone.
- **Daily correlation with the neighbour:** min 0.72, p10 0.93.
- **Scale:** 0.74–1.17.
- **p1 CF over 2007–13:** 0.125 → 0.180 (about 0.178 expected).
- **Clipping:** at most about 0.15% of energy lost; 1.9% of hours clipped, see below.
- **Output sha256:** fb0fda93… The folder moves to `pg_data/profiles_pvfix`.

**Setting:** `pv_profile_repair: {mode: neighbour_fill, profiles_dir: pg_data/profiles_pvfix, verify: manifest,
neighbour_method: correlation, output_sha256: fb0fda93}`. It changes results: PV in the zeroed zones, p1 most.

**New build checks** (`production.apply_pv_profile_repair`):
- `neighbour_method` must equal the manifest's.
- `output_sha256`, at least 8 hex digits, must prefix the manifest's recorded output sha256. A manifest without one
  (a run without `--hash`) is refused.
- Both compare the manifest only; nothing is rehashed. The existing size and mtime check still ties the manifest to the
  file.
- Replace the prefix with the full hash when convenient.
- **Fix:** `write_pv_repair_log` warns instead of failing when a manifest has no fill log.

**Clipping (runbook item a):**
- **What the run showed:** clipped hours were 1.9% of filled hours. Each is a scale above 1 applied to a neighbour hour
  already at the inverter limit, so the share of hours says little about the energy lost.
- **Recorded per site-year now:** `write_repaired` records the output cut (`clipped_energy_lost`) and the scaled
  output before the cut (`scaled_energy`). The manifest gains `clipped_energy_share` and `clipped_hours_share`.
- **The runbook check:** `clipped_energy_share` ≤ 0.5%, review above 0.25%. Clipped hours are information only.
- **The 12 Oct manifest:** it predates these fields; the dashboards' figure (about 0.15%) stands for that run.

**Runbook (items b and c):**
- **Measured resources:** 6 h with `--hash`, 17.4 GB peak memory, a repaired solar file of about 10 GB (2.3 × the
  source: the filled years compress less).
- **Links:** the other files (wind profiles, mappings) can be hard links, the default on the same volume.
- **Moving the folder:** use `mv`, `rsync -a` or `cp -a`, because the build checks the file's mtime.
- **Section 6 (future, not done):** vectorise the 2007–11 write, the slow step.

**The regression row (`on_pgdays`):** sets `pv_profile_repair` back to off, so it still resolves to v3.1 (SHARED_CHANGES
row 131).

**Flags-off patch** (`s0_workflow/data/v32/v32_flags_off.patch`, §130): now 8 changes, the PV repair added. It still
parses to 4696653's settings.

**Tests:**
- `test_pv_profile_repair.py`: the yml value; method and hash checks (match, prefix, uppercase, wrong method, wrong
  hash, too short, no `--hash`); clipped energy share.
- `conftest.py`: a stub manifest for the default folder while it is missing, as for the offshore folder (§129).
- Regression row, S-set and flags-off patch tests updated.

**§132 addendum (2026-10-13): the full hash is recorded.** `pv_profile_repair.output_sha256` is now the full value in
place of the 8-digit prefix.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Output sha256 | fb0fda93f25276a531226456614856c4d9218e71e92b303cce8f4849ba497057 |
| Size | 9,860,071,492 bytes |
| mtime_ns | 1791824435248602700 |
| Source sha256 | 426cb61f64c46ad8262f2207a2ef46710d19978cd0fb91969bdc5a3f42358173 |

- **Confirmed by the dashboards:** a fresh hash of the file matches. The folder is at `pg_data/profiles_pvfix`, and
  `apply_pv_profile_repair` at 7f1b384 passes against it with the full hash.
- **Wind files:** the folder's 5 wind files are now hard links to `pg_data/profiles`, checked by sha256 before linking.
  The manifest still lists them under `other_files_copied`, a record field no check reads; the runbook notes it.
- **Follow-up:** the flags-off patch and the PV repair test now carry the full hash.
