## 134. S0 v3.2 Blockers: Offshore Split on the Real Layout, Cluster-Cache Guard, PowerGenome Import (V32-04)

**Date:** 2026-10-13. **Branch:** `tom/s0-v3.2`. From the runner's two blockers, both due before the v3.2 builds.

### A. Offshore split (recipe L rewritten; `s0_workflow/offshore_groups.py`, `scripts/split_offshore_groups.py`)

**The real layout:**
- ReEDS-cpas-patched has four JSONs, `offshorewind_{fixed,floating}_{false,true}.json`. Each has its own
  `turbine_type` and `pref_site`, and all point at one `offshorewind_lcoe_ReEDS.csv` (39,005 CPAs).
- Per-type CSVs (13,108 / 4,215 / 20,054 / 1,628 rows) sit in the folder unused.
- The §120 script split one JSON by metadata columns, so on this layout the other three JSONs would point at a file
  left out of the copy. The manifest check would still pass, and PowerGenome would load the broken groups.

**Fix (`--map JSON=CSV`, one per JSON):**
- The per-type CSVs are used as membership lists only. The script stops unless they partition the shared file exactly
  (every CPA_ID once) and every JSON that uses the shared file is mapped.
- Each JSON is pointed at a new metadata file holding the shared file's rows for its members.
- **Why the shared rows:** `patch_pg_resource_groups.py` rewrote only the shared file (ipm_region_y → ipm_region, CPAs
  nearest MD/RI moved to p123/p133, lat/lon added). The per-type CSVs predate it, and pointing at them would undo the
  MD/RI fix. The manifest counts the rows whose zone differs from the shared file (`zone_differs_from_shared`).
- **`--survey`** lists the offshore JSONs and the CSVs next to them, for the runner to fill the map.

**Reference check:** `check_references` (every JSON under the folder must reference files that exist).
- PowerGenome loads `**/*.json`, so every JSON counts.
- It runs at the end of the split (stop on failure) and in the build's `check_folder` (stop).

**The case check, per group:** `check_case` compares each zone's limits with the true resource of the groups the
case's technologies come from, read from the `_fixed_1` / `_fixed_0` / `_floating_0` name suffixes. It falls back to
all groups when the names don't carry them.

**FOR TOM:** `resources.yml` asks for fixed/1, fixed/0 and floating/0 only. After the split, the floating/true group
(1,628 CPAs) is in no cluster entry and is not offered. In v3.1 those CPAs were inside every group. Adding an entry is
a scenario decision, so it isn't made here; recipe L step 3 reports the group's GW.

### A3. Cluster cache (`s0_workflow/build_guard.py`)

- **Does the key change on its own?** Not determinable from this repo; PowerGenome's code is off limits, and the
  README's rule is to clear the cache when resource groups change. Treated as no.
- **The rule:** move `pg/extra_inputs/cluster_assignments` aside before the first v3.2 build. No need to identify the
  offshore folders, and the PV repair needs the move anyway.
- **The guard** (`check_cluster_cache`, in `apply_settings`):
  - With the PV repair or the offshore split on, the cache must be empty or missing the first time. It then gets
    `S0_V32_CACHE.json`, holding the PV sha256 and the split manifest's sha256.
  - Later builds need the same fingerprint.
  - A flags-off build refuses a cache with that marker.
  - A v3.1 cache (entries, no marker) is refused by a v3.2 build.
- **Why not modification times:** v3.1 S-set builds still running could write entries newer than the PV manifest.

### B. PowerGenome import (V32-04)

**The setting:** `s0_production.powergenome_commit: c4c433cfefd0ae11f5b3f47ae05e3b9d7c90a691`. Once per build,
`build_guard.check_powergenome`:
- logs the imported package's folder, git commit, uncommitted changes and whether
  `data/cost_multipliers/AEO_2026_regional_cost_corrections.csv` is there;
- stops unless the commit matches and the file is present;
- writes `powergenome_provenance.json` to every case, as the proof.

**Import:** `PYTHONPATH=<worktree>\PowerGenome` (recipe L step 6). It precedes site-packages and editable installs.

**Exemptions:** the flags-off patch (now 9 changes) and the regression row (`on_pgdays`) set `powergenome_commit: ~`,
which records the import without enforcing it.

**Second flags-off card (F2, `v32_run_cards.md`):** F1 with the submodule imported, to isolate the pin. c4c433c is
5ecb454 plus that one data file, so F2 should equal F1 byte for byte if the Q3 checkout is 5ecb454 with the same CSV.
Otherwise the provenance files show where they differ: the CSV's sha256 (regional capex in `gen_build_costs.csv`) or
the commit.

**Tests:**
- `test_offshore_groups.py`:
  - the type-file split keeps the patched rows;
  - an overlapping, incomplete or unmapped partition is refused;
  - a broken reference fails `check_folder`;
  - the case check covers the requested groups;
  - the CLI survey and map.
- `test_build_guard.py`: the commit check and provenance; the cache marker in each state.
- `conftest.py`: the cache sandbox and a PowerGenome stub where it isn't installed.
- The flags-off patch and S-set tests are updated.

## 135. S0 v3.2 Handoff: Run Cards and READY Files in the Repo

**Date:** 2026-10-13. **Branch:** `tom/s0-v3.2`.

**Where:** `s0_workflow/runcards/v32/`, so the VM runner works from the repo overnight. The folder holds:
- `recipe_L.md`: §134's recipe, plus the script fix and the missing-metadata check;
- `flagsoff_build2.md` (F2);
- `s0_v32.md`: the chain card, with the commit, the c4c433c import, the proving log line and the pre-solve checks;
- the seven scenario cards;
- `README.md`.

**READY files:**
- **`READY_S0_V32`:** one line, the commit the chain must run at. It is written in a later commit, because a file
  can't name its own commit.
- **`READY_SCENARIOS`:** written only once the scenario cards are final. **Not yet written:** S1, S2 and S3 wait on
  Tom's storage-credit decision (§133). The scenarios can't start before S0 v3.2 is solved, so holding the file costs
  no VM time.

**Reuse code check:** `chain_reuse.CODE_EXEMPT` gains `s0_workflow/runcards/*`. Commits after the chain's commit that
touch only the cards and READY files don't count as code changes, so they don't stop the scenarios' reuse of S0 v3.2's
stages. Test: `test_chain_reuse.py::test_runcards_are_not_code`.

**Gate in `s0_v32.md` (pending Tom):** the floating/pref-site offshore group (1,628 CPAs) isn't offered after the
split (§134). The runner stops before solving if that group's GW at LCOE ≤ 125 exceeds 1% of the true resource
(29,855 MW), and otherwise reports the figure and continues.

## 136. S0 v3.2: S1–S3 Storage Credit Flat to 2045 (Tom's Decision), Credit Report by Technology, READY_SCENARIOS

**Date:** 2026-10-13. **Branch:** `tom/s0-v3.2`. Resolves §133's conflict, with option (b).

**1. Storage credit in S1, S2 and S3 (credit restoration).**
- **Decision:** the storage credit is extended alongside the reinstated wind and solar credits, flat 30% through 2045
  as in v3.1. S0 v3.2, BILL_central, txonly, S4 and S5 keep the OBBBA phase-down.
- **The change:** each of the three `s_set` values in `pg/settings/scenario_management.yml` adds
  `storage_credit: {phase_down: {2034: 1.0, 2035: 1.0, 2036: 1.0}}`. Battery capex is ×0.70 in every stage.
- **Why this form, not `mode: flat`:**
  - **Same writer as S0 v3.2.** `mode` stays `obbba`, so the credit goes through the same investment-credit writer as
    S0. The 2028 stage (phase 1.0 in both, since the phase-down starts in 2034) then gets the same settings and the
    same `gen_build_costs.csv` and `investment_credit_report.csv` as S0 v3.2's. The report records only each period's
    phase, never the schedule.
  - **What `mode: flat` would do instead:** put the ×0.7 back in the ATB modifier and drop the report file, so S1's
    2028 inputs would differ from S0's and its 2028 reuse would be lost.
- **Card headers:** S1, S2 and S3 say "storage credit: flat 30% to 2045 (credit restoration); S0 v3.2 baseline uses
  the phase-down, so S1–S0 now includes the storage-credit extension".
- **2028 reuse:**
  - S1's 2028 inputs should still be byte-identical to S0 v3.2's, so its 2028 reuse holds.
  - S2 and S3 never reused 2028 (their build rate and headroom differ from 2028, as in v3.1); the storage change
    doesn't alter that.
- **Comparator fix (`chain_reuse._at_year`):** a `phase_down` before its first key now collapses to 1.0 (full credit),
  the writer's rule. It used to take the first key's value (0.75), which would have predicted S1's 2028 as different.
  `chain_reuse_expected.csv` is unchanged.

**2. Credits paid, split by technology** (`s0_workflow/credit_spend.py`, `scripts/credit_spend.py`).
- **`storage_itc`:** per stage, the 48E ITC on the storage built in that period, a one-time amount. It is the
  after-credit capex (MW × `gen_overnight_cost` + MWh × `gen_storage_energy_overnight_cost`) × f/(1−f), where
  f = ITC rate × phase from `investment_credit_report.csv`.
- **The summary:** keeps v3.1's columns (the three categories and `total`: the wind and solar production credits, on
  the basis of v3.1's statutory cost, S1 $664bn and S2 $768bn to 2045). It adds `wind_per_yr`, `solar_per_yr` and
  `storage_itc_one_time`. The script also writes `<out>_storage_itc.csv`.
- **Who runs it:** every scenario card and the S0 v3.2 card (the baseline for S1–S0) run it after the solve.
- **Test:** `test_s_set.py::test_storage_itc_and_technology_split`.

**3. v4 item (bucket B, supply inputs): "add the fourth offshore cluster entry before any offshore sensitivity".**
- The floating/pref-site=1 group (1,628 CPAs) has no cluster entry in `resources.yml`, so after the split (§134) it
  isn't offered. Tom accepted the drop for S0 v3.2, with the 1% gate in `s0_v32.md`.
- Housekeeping's v4 numbering isn't in this repo or its branches, so the ID is for housekeeping to assign.

**4. READY_SCENARIOS:** the seven cards are final (header notes, `--code-check model` required, the credit report
step). `READY_SCENARIOS` names the commit for each card. The S0 v3.2 card (`READY_S0_V32`, a4de740) is unchanged
apart from the post-solve credit report step.

## 137. S0 v3.2: Recipe L Incident Fixed; Reference Check as PowerGenome Resolves; Fourth Offshore Group; READY Re-Issued

**Date:** 2026-10-13. **Branch:** `tom/s0-v3.2`. `READY_S0_V32` (a4de740) and `READY_SCENARIOS` (b9d677c) were
withdrawn; this commit deletes both. They are re-issued in the next commit, at this commit.

**1. Incident (recipe L, §134).**
- **What happened:** the card had the split folder written inside the shared `pg_data\resource_groups`. That is every
  worktree's `RESOURCE_GROUPS`, and PowerGenome loads every `**/*.json` under it. From 04:37 to about 10:40 UTC any
  build on the shared data could have crashed on `offshore_split_manifest.json`, or counted offshore twice.
- **The runner's containment:** the folder was moved to `ic_test_fedpol_work\recipeL_held\`; the source groups are
  untouched.
- **Fixes:**
  - **A v3.2 root beside the shared one:** the split now writes a full, hard-linked copy of the `RESOURCE_GROUPS` root
    to `pg_data/resource_groups_v32`, with the four offshore JSONs rewritten.
    `s0_production.offshore_filter_fix.resource_groups_dir` points v3.2 S0 builds at it through the settings, so
    only those builds see it. No `env.yml` changes; a flags-off build with a split folder as `RESOURCE_GROUPS` is
    refused.
  - **The manifest beside the copy:** `offshore_split_manifest.json` and `offshore_true_resource_by_zone.csv` go in
    `<folder>__meta` (`offshore_groups.meta_dir`), outside anything PowerGenome globs. `check_folder` stops if a
    manifest is found inside the folder.
  - **A pre-write check** (`offshore_groups.guard_output`): the script refuses any output that overlaps `--groups-dir`,
    `env.yml`'s `RESOURCE_GROUPS` / `RESOURCE_GROUP_PROFILES`, or any `--shared-root`. The build applies the same rule
    to `resource_groups_dir` against the roots it scans. The card rule is in `recipe_L.md` ("RULE").
  - **One recipe:** `VM_RECIPES.md` recipe L now points at `s0_workflow/runcards/v32/recipe_L.md`.

**2. Reference-check false positive.** The reV parquet profiles live in `pg_data/profiles`
(`RESOURCE_GROUP_PROFILES`), not next to the JSONs. `check_references` now resolves files as PowerGenome does:
- `metadata` must sit next to its JSON;
- other data files (profiles, site maps) may sit next to it or in a profiles root: `env.yml`'s
  `RESOURCE_GROUP_PROFILES` in the script (`--profiles-root` to add more), and the build's own
  `RESOURCE_GROUP_PROFILES` in the build.

**3. Fourth offshore group (floating, preferred sites; 147.8 GW at LCOE ≤ 125 on the VM, against the 29.9 GW gate).**
- **The fix:** with the split on, the build (`offshore_groups.add_floating_pref_entries`) adds a floating /
  pref-site-1 cluster entry for every floating / pref-site-0 entry. `load_settings` has already expanded these per
  region, and the copies keep the same filter and bins.
- **Unchanged:** `resources.yml`, so v3.1 and flags-off builds are unaffected.
- **The case check** requires every group with resource (all four) to be offered (`missing_groups`), and compares
  limits zone by zone with the four groups' true resource.
- **Tags and mandates:** the `OffShoreWind` resource tags and state mandates match the new technologies as they do the
  other three.
- **Retired:** the 1% gate in `s0_v32.md`. §136's v4 item ("add the fourth offshore cluster entry before any offshore
  sensitivity") is done in v3.2 and no longer needed.

**4. Storage credit in S1–S3 (Tom: option b; as §136).**
- The card headers now read "storage credit: flat 30% to 2045 (credit restoration); S0 v3.2 uses the phase-down, so
  S1–S0 includes the storage extension".
- The credit report split (wind / solar / storage ITC) and S1's byte-identical 2028 inputs stand as in §136. The
  fourth offshore entry applies to S0 v3.2 and every scenario alike.

**Flags-off patch:** regenerated for the new `offshore_filter_fix` line (9 changes; it still parses to 4696653's
settings).

**Tests (`test_offshore_groups.py`):**
- output inside a scanned root refused: the incident's path, another root, and `env.yml`'s root;
- the manifest outside the copy;
- a stray manifest inside the folder fails;
- profiles resolved under the profiles root;
- the fourth entry added, idempotently, and the four groups required.

Updated: `test_build_guard.py` and `conftest.py`.

## 138. S0 v3.2: Path Settings Kept as Paths (Cold-Cache Build Failure)

**Date:** 2026-10-13. **Branch:** `tom/s0-v3.2`.

**The failure:** the S0 v3.2 build at c0759bf failed after 4 minutes on a cold cluster cache, with
`AttributeError: 'str' object has no attribute 'as_posix'` at PowerGenome `nrelatb.py:1734`. It had already passed the
offshore check (2,985.5 GW, 134 floating/pref-site cluster entries added).

**Cause:** `apply_offshore_filter_fix` set `s["RESOURCE_GROUPS"] = str(...)`. PowerGenome's own settings carry it as a
`Path` and call `.as_posix()` on it when they write a new cache entry. `apply_pv_profile_repair` did the same with
`RESOURCE_GROUP_PROFILES`, the next trap on the same path. The F builds never reached either line: they ran with the
flags off, on the warm v3.1 cache.

**Fix:** both are set as resolved `Path` objects. Nothing else changes.

**Test** (`test_pg_path_settings.py`):
- `apply_settings` on a v3.2 case with a real split folder, a real repaired PV folder and a cold (missing) cache:
  every path setting stays a `Path` and answers `.as_posix()`, as PowerGenome's cache writer needs; the cold cache
  gets its marker; the fourth offshore entry is added.
- A flags-off case leaves the path settings with the same values and types.

**Scope:**
- **Only the flags-on paths change:** the split on (`apply_offshore_filter_fix`) and the PV repair on
  (`apply_pv_profile_repair`).
- **Flags-off builds are unaffected:** both functions return before those lines when their flag is off, so the
  flags-off certification of c0759bf (F3) holds.
- **The split code** (`offshore_groups.py`, `split_offshore_groups.py`) is not touched, and the split folder needn't be
  redone.

## 139. S0 v3.2: Day-Weights LP Infeasibility Diagnosis; READY Files Withdrawn

**Date:** 2026-10-13. **Branch:** `tom/s0-v3.2`.

**The failure:** the S0 v3.2 build at 0c9d9f7 (past §138) stopped in the 2028 day selection, with 24 days:
`fleet-independent day weights LP infeasible even with tolerances x2.25 (HiGHS status 8)`. F1–F3 (flags off) passed this
step.

### Diagnosis mode (`s0_workflow/day_selection.py`)

- **When it runs:** only after the last relaxation step fails, just before the error is raised. In day mode it runs on
  the candidate days; in block mode, on the candidate set with the lowest relaxation bound. A feasible LP never reaches
  it, so feasible selections are unchanged.
- **What it does:** it re-solves the failed LP, at the final relaxation, with elastic slack on each constraint family:
  - **target:** each side of each target, in target units, scaled by the base tolerance;
  - **tail:** each side of each tail band, in % of hours, scaled by the base band width;
  - **w_min:** each day's minimum weight, in days/yr, scaled by `w_min`;
  - **weight_sum:** each side of the weight total, in days/yr, scaled by `w_min`.
  It minimises the scaled slack sum: once with every family elastic (`all`), then once with each family alone
  (`only <family>`). A family whose run alone is infeasible can't explain the failure by itself.
- **What it writes,** in `<case>/time_sampling/<year>/`:
  - `fi_elastic_slack.csv`: one row per nonzero slack (run, family, constraint, side, amount, unit, the current limit
    and the limit needed), and one row per infeasible run;
  - `fi_target_range.csv`: whether each target lies within what the candidate days can reach, whatever the weights;
  - `fi_cluster_profile_stats.csv`: per wind or solar cluster, its kind, mean, min, max and share of zero hours, with
    a flag for degenerate profiles (constant, mean near zero, or outside [0, 1]), and whether it enters the targets;
  - `fi_failed_lp.npz`: the failed LP.
  The error message ends with a summary of these files.
- **What-ifs without a rebuild:** `s0_workflow/scripts/diagnose_day_weights.py <npz>` reruns the diagnosis, with
  `--drop-tail`, `--drop-target`, `--tol-scale <text>=<factor>`, `--relax` or `--w-min`.

### Review: degenerate fleets in the targets and tails

- **What the LP uses:**
  - **Targets:** load, onshore-wind CF and utility-solar CF, per transreg and national. Each is the mean over that
    kind's clusters.
  - **Tails:** the top 1% national load hours, and the bottom 1% net-load hours of the stylised fleet. S0 sets
    `low_net_load_fleets: []`, so the stylised fleet is built from the same onshore-wind and solar series.
  - `kind()` maps every offshore technology to `offwind`, which is in neither the targets nor the tails. That includes
    the fourth group, `…_floating_1`.
- **So the floating_1 group can't change the LP,** however degenerate its profile. There is nothing to exclude or
  merge. The test adds the four offshore groups, with floating_1 all-zero, near-zero and constant, and the days,
  weights, target errors and tail shares are identical.
- **`cf_fidelity` rescale can't change it either:** it runs after day selection (`pg_to_switch.py`).
- **Of the v3.2 changes, two remain:**
  - the PV repair, which changes the solar CF targets and, through them, the stylised fleet's bottom-1% hours;
  - the cold cluster cache (§138), which reclusters wind and solar.
  The F builds used the warm v3.1 cache. A toggle test with the PV repair off also needs a cold cache: the cache guard
  refuses the v3.2 cache for other data.
- **Degenerate profiles in the targets:**
  - an all-zero or constant onshore-wind or solar cluster dilutes its transreg's mean CF. It can't make the LP
    infeasible by itself, since a constant series is met by any weights summing to the year;
  - what can make it infeasible is a target the candidate days can't reach (`fi_target_range.csv`) or a CF outside
    [0, 1], since the tolerances are absolute (`fi_cluster_profile_stats.csv`).
  - The fix will follow the culprit. A principled exclusion is preferred over a loosened tolerance, and must leave
    flags-off results unchanged.

### READY files withdrawn

- `READY_S0_V32` and `READY_SCENARIOS` named 0c9d9f7, which fails. Both are deleted.
- They come back together, at one commit, once the fix is in.

**Tests** (`test_day_selection_diagnosis.py`):
- the offshore and degenerate clusters change nothing, and a feasible run writes no diagnosis files;
- an infeasible case (`w_min` × days above the year): only the `w_min` family absorbs it, the slack covers the excess,
  the script reproduces the table, and the `--w-min 1` what-if needs no slack;
- an unreachable target is named with its exact gap;
- the diagnosis is only on the failure path.

**Scope:**
- Nothing a feasible build writes changes, so the flags-off certification (F3) holds.
- `s0_workflow/` is reuse code, so a build at this commit and one at 0c9d9f7 differ in their code hash. Use
  `--code-check model` if both are mixed.

## 240. S0 v3.2: 2028 Day Selection Fixed by Swapping Cluster Representatives; READY Files Re-Issued

**Date:** 2026-10-13. **Branch:** `tom/s0-v3.2`. **Numbering:** the S0 line's block §113–139 is used up; this line
continues at §240–259.

### The cause (the VM's probes and the §139 diagnosis, 2028 only)

| Probe (at 0c9d9f7) | Cluster cache | Result |
|---|---|---|
| A: as built | v3.2 | infeasible at ×2.25 |
| B: offshore split off | cold | infeasible at ×2.25 |
| C: PV repair off | cold | feasible |

- **The PV repair causes it, indirectly.** C reclustered from an empty cache and passed, so the cold cache alone isn't
  the cause; B rules out the split.
- **What fails is FRCC's onshore-wind target, not a solar one** (`fi_elastic_slack.csv`): it needs tolerance ×2.515
  against ×2.25. The tail and minimum-weight families can't absorb it.
- **It is a joint conflict:** every target, FRCC wind included (0.267 against a reachable 0.043–0.506), can be
  reached alone (`fi_target_range.csv`).
- **The mechanism:** the repair changes the solar shapes that feed k-means. So the candidate days change, and the 49
  targets no longer fit together on the new medoids.
- **No degenerate fleet:** no profile is constant, near zero, NaN or all-zero (`fi_cluster_profile_stats.csv`). The 12
  utility-PV clusters flagged there peak at 1.000001–1.000015, a rounding excess. The same 65 profiles reach the top of
  the range with the repair off, and C passed. The flag now ignores an excess under 1e-4.
- **Not floating_1 and not `cf_fidelity`:** neither enters the selection (§139).

### The fix (`s0_workflow/day_selection.py`, `_swap_representatives`)

**When it applies:** only after the medoids fail at every relaxation step, where the build used to stop. A selection
that succeeds on its medoids never reaches it.

**What it does:**
- A k-means medoid is one member of its cluster, and the members nearest its centre are its closest alternatives.
- For each step of the usual schedule (×1, ×1.5, ×2.25), it swaps medoids, one at a time, for one of the 10 members of
  their own cluster nearest its centre, at most 3 swaps in all.
- If some swap makes the weights LP feasible, the search ends with the feasible set that has the lowest weights-LP
  objective. Otherwise it keeps the swap that most reduces the elastic slack (§139) and goes on.
- The first step at which it succeeds is the one used, so the reported `relax_factor` is the tightest that works.

**What it never changes:**
- the targets (all 49 are kept);
- the tails and their bands;
- `w_min`;
- the tolerances and the relaxation schedule;
- the top-load, low-net-load and peak days (never swapped out or in).

**Why this rather than an exclusion or a merge:**
- FRCC wind isn't degenerate. Dropping its target would drop a fleet the model builds, and a tolerance of ×2.515 is a
  loosening.
- The medoid is a convention of k-means, not a requirement of the selection. Another member of the same cluster
  represents that cluster's days.

**What it writes:**
- `fi_candidate_swaps.csv` (relaxation step, tolerance factor, cluster, from day, to day, alternate rank, elastic
  slack after);
- `candidate_swaps` in `fi_info.json`;
- the role `cluster member (swapped for the medoid)` in `fi_days_selected.csv`;
- a console warning;
- the medoids' diagnosis (§139) is still written.

**If no swap helps,** the build stops as before, with the §139 diagnosis.

**Cost:** up to about 6 s per year on a 24-day, 35-target fixture when every swap fails. The swap runs only in a year
whose medoids fail.

### Flags-off results unchanged (F3)

- Nothing changes for a selection whose medoids are feasible. No setting is added to `ts`, so `fi_info.json` is unchanged
  too.
- **Checked against F3's code** (0c9d9f7's `day_selection.py`) on 12 synthetic records that pass on their medoids.
  Every output was byte-identical: the selected days, weights, profiles and all five `fi_*` files.
- F1–F3 passed day selection on their medoids, so their builds are unchanged.

### Test (`test_day_selection_swap.py`): the VM's case, reproduced on a synthetic record

The record has eleven transregs, giving 35 targets for 15 weighted days.
- **"Unrepaired":** solar is zeroed on 8% of days in three western regions. It passes on its medoids, with no swaps and
  no diagnosis.
- **"Repaired" (filled):** with swaps off (`swap_max: 0`), it stops as on the VM. The only slack is one regional wind
  target (below), every target is reachable alone, and neither tails nor `w_min` can absorb it.
- **"Repaired", with the fix:**
  - feasible after at most 3 swaps;
  - every target kept and within tolerance, base tolerances unchanged;
  - tail bands met and weights at least `w_min`;
  - the tail and peak days untouched;
  - deterministic.
- **Every swap stays in its own k-means cluster.**

### READY files re-issued

`READY_S0_V32` and `READY_SCENARIOS` are written in the next commit. Both name the commit with this fix.

**Card change:** `s0_v32.md`, pre-solve check 12, expects the swap warning in 2028 and asks for the swaps and
`relax_factor` per stage.

**SHARED_CHANGES:** no row. Only `s0_workflow/` changes; row 133 stays free.

## 241. S0 v3.2: Offshore Check by Stage (No Floating Before 2030); Sweep of v3.2 Checks for Year and Scenario Assumptions

**Date:** 2026-10-13. **Branch:** `tom/s0-v3.2`.

**The failure:** the S0 v3.2 build at 4bdbcad passed day selection (2028: 2 representatives swapped, feasible at
×1.000). It then stopped writing the 2028 case inputs, at `production.py` `write_offshore_check`, with:
`offshore_filter_fix: groups with resource but no offshore technology in the case: [('floating', 0), ('floating', 1)]`.

**Cause:**
- `scenario_management.yml` gives 2026–2029 an `atb_new_gen` without `[OffShoreWind, Class12, …]`: no floating
  offshore before 2030 (no ATB prices). So 2028 has only `fixed_0` (65 entries), `fixed_1` (25) and existing offshore
  (4), as v3.1 and F3 did.
- The §137 check required all four groups in every stage.

### The fix (`offshore_groups.py`, `production.write_offshore_check`)

**What the check now uses:** `og.offered_groups(settings)` gives the (turbine type, pref site) groups a stage offers.
These are the offshore `renewables_clusters` entries whose turbine type has an `OffShoreWind` technology in that stage's
`atb_new_gen`, read after the `scenario_management` overrides. ATB classes 1–7 are fixed and 8–14 floating
(`resources.yml`: Class3 fixed, Class12 floating). `write_case_inputs` passes the stage's settings, and a multi-period
case folder takes the union over its years.

**What the build requires:**
- Only the groups the stage offers, and only where they have resource in a zone of the case. 2028 requires `fixed_0`
  and `fixed_1`; 2030 on requires all four.
- A case technology from a group the stage doesn't offer stops the build (e.g. floating in 2028).
- A stage with no new offshore technology at all now goes through the check too, with the split on. It used to return
  before checking anything. A `gen_info.csv` without capacity limits (`gen_capacity_limit_mw`; unit-test cases only)
  still returns at once: there is nothing to compare.
- Each zone's limits are still compared with the true resource of the groups the case has, as before.

**What the log adds:** the groups not offered in the stage, with their GW, e.g. 2028: `not offered this stage
(atb_new_gen): [('floating', 0), ('floating', 1)], … GW`.

**Unchanged:** called without the stage's settings, the check behaves as §137.

**`fixed_1` count (for the runner):** 2028 has `fixed_1` in 25 entries, against 72 in v3.1. That is expected after the
split: preferred-site fixed exists only where there are preferred fixed-bottom CPAs. In v3.1 every offshore entry took
every CPA. `s0_v32.md` check 4 says so.

### Sweep: v3.2 code and checks that could assume a technology, group or setting in every stage or scenario

Cases checked: S0prod_A, BILL_central, txonly, S1–S5, in 2028, 2030, 2035, 2040 and 2045.

| § | Item | Varies by | Verdict |
|---|---|---|---|
| 120, 129, 134, 137 | Offshore check: all four groups required | year (floating from 2030) | **Bug, fixed here.** Also covers a stage with no new offshore, zones outside the case, and a group in the case that its settings don't offer |
| 137 | `add_floating_pref_entries` | year | Safe. It adds the entries in every year. PowerGenome builds them only where `atb_new_gen` has Class12, as for floating/pref-site-0 in v3.1 (the warning `resources.yml` documents) |
| 137 | `check_references`, `check_folder`, `guard_output` | — | Safe. They check the split folder once and don't depend on year |
| 113, 126, 136 | Storage credit `obbba` (credit writer term) | year, scenario (the `tax_credits` axis changes in 2030 for S1–S3) | Safe. It is read per case-year from that year's battery modifier; a year with no battery credit gets no term. The writer works per period and skips a period with no term. `phase_down` before its first key collapses to 1.0 (§136; `test_s_set_on_v32` covers every case and year) |
| 136 | Credit report `storage_itc` | stage | Safe: a stage with no report is skipped. **Hardened here:** a report written without rows (a credit that matched no project) is read as "no report" instead of failing |
| 136 | Credit report `summary` | stage, technology | Safe: missing wind, solar or storage rows give 0 |
| 114 | CA free tranche | year | Safe: written per period, and a period without a CA price is skipped |
| 127 | WA `per_mwh` (two-tranche off) | year | Safe: the same per-period loop |
| 116 | `cf_fidelity` rescale | year (sampled days) | Safe: it rescales the clusters present; a kind absent from a stage is skipped; a cluster with no output on the sampled days is flagged, not rescaled |
| 118 | `prm_temp_tz_fix` | — | Safe: it changes only the time zone temperatures are read in |
| 119 | `startup_cost_fix` check | year, scenario | Safe: with no new gas it logs and passes; the CCS variants (`clean_power_regs`) have keys; no v3.2 case uses the CCS axis |
| 121–125 | Build-rate package R1, R11, R2, R4, R7 | first stage, scenario (S3/S5: no build rate) | Safe, and off in every v3.2 case. A first stage with no previous stage skips the ramp; R11 skips without a previous build; a case without a build rate doesn't load the module |
| 126 | Wind price factor 1.0 | scenario (`techmax`, the bill's switch to `atts_reform` in 2035) | Safe: written per case-year from that year's generators, and a stage with no wind writes no file. The test checks 1.0 in all seven cases and five years. No scenario sets wind's weight above 1.0, the only way it could be refused |
| 132, 138 | PV repair folder | — | Safe: the same data in every year; the log is copied to every stage |
| 134 | Cluster-cache guard | year | Safe: the first stage writes the marker and later stages match it |
| 134 | PowerGenome provenance | — | Safe: checked once per build |
| 139 | Day-selection diagnosis | year | Safe: it runs per year, only when the LP fails |
| 240 | Representative swap | year | Safe: per year, only when the medoids fail. Other years may swap too; the card treats that as normal |
| 130 | Flags-off patch | — | Safe: settings only |
| 133, 135 | Run cards | year | **Fixed here:** `s0_v32.md` check 4 and `recipe_L.md` expected floating in every stage. They now give 2028 (fixed only) and 2030 on (all four), with the `fixed_1` count note. The scenario cards now point to check 12 (day selection) too. `v32_run_cards.md` qualifies its "ratio 1.00 in every zone" |

### Tests (`test_offshore_year_aware.py`)

- **Settings, every case and stage:** the merged settings of all eight cases in all five stages (resources.yml, the
  year's `all_cases`, the case's axes) offer fixed only in 2028 and all four groups from 2030.
- **A 2028-like stage:**
  - `fixed_0` and `fixed_1` only: it passes, the floating groups are logged as not offered, and the limits are still
    compared;
  - the same case without the stage's settings stops as the VM's build did;
  - a doubled `fixed_1` limit stops it;
  - a floating technology in the case stops it.
- **A 2030-like stage:** all four groups pass; missing `floating_1` stops it; fixed only stops it; a 2028+2030 case
  takes the union.
- **No new offshore:** with groups offered, it stops; with none offered, it passes; zones outside the case aren't
  required.
- **Flags off:** the report is the same with or without the stage's settings.
- **Credit report:** an empty investment report reads as no credit.

### Flags-off certification (F3) holds

Since F3's commit (0c9d9f7), the code a build runs changed in two places:
- `day_selection.py` (§139, §240): it runs only when the weights LP fails. On 12 records that pass on their medoids,
  the outputs were again byte-identical to F3's code.
- `production.write_offshore_check` (§241): with the split off, the same code runs, and the test shows the same output
  with or without the stage's settings.

`credit_spend.py` runs after solves only. F1–F3 inputs are unchanged.

**READY files:** `READY_S0_V32` and `READY_SCENARIOS` are re-issued in the next commit, both naming the commit with this
fix. **SHARED_CHANGES:** no row; row 133 stays free.

## 242. S0 v3.2: Offshore Limit Check Allows the 0.1 MW Rounding

**Date:** 2026-10-14. **Branch:** `tom/s0-v3.2`.

**The failure:** the S0 v3.2 build at e135750 stopped in the 2028 offshore check, on p10 alone.

**Cause (the runner's diagnosis):**
- p10 has one new offshore project (`fixed_0`) with a capacity limit of 5.5 MW, against a true resource of 5.4536 MW in
  the manifest.
- All 90 offshore limits in the case are whole multiples of 0.1 MW.
- `check_case` allowed only `0.001 × max(true, 1 MW)`, which is 0.0055 MW for p10. The 0.046 MW rounding gap exceeds
  it. Larger zones absorb the same rounding within the relative tolerance.

**Fix (`offshore_groups.check_case`):** the allowed difference per zone is now the larger of the relative tolerance
and half a rounding step (0.05 MW) per offshore project in the zone. `offshore_mw_by_zone.csv` gains `projects` and
`allowed_mw`.

**It still catches duplication:** the resource offered twice or three times is far outside the allowance. In p10, ×3
is 16.5 MW against 5.45 ± 0.15. Only a zone with under about 0.075 MW of true resource per project could hide a
tripling inside the rounding.

**Tests** (`test_offshore_year_aware.py`):
- p10 as on the VM passes, and the old rule (no rounding allowance) refuses it;
- a project 0.2 MW over stops the build;
- p10 offered three times stops the build (×3.03).

**Flags off (F3):** `check_case` runs only with the split on, so F1–F3 are unchanged.

**Card:** `s0_v32.md` check 4 explains the new columns.

**SHARED_CHANGES:** no row; row 133 stays free.

## 243. S0 v3.2: Resuming an Interrupted Chain (`resume_chain.py`, RESUME.md); READY Re-Issued

**Date:** 2026-10-14. **Branch:** `tom/s0-v3.2`.

**What happened:**
- The S0 v3.2 build finished cleanly at 4894d6a at 02:40 UTC, and all 88 pre-solve checks passed.
- The chain started at 02:42 UTC (five stages, no reuse).
- The VM shut down overnight, and the runner session was lost. How many stages solved is unknown.

**The chain runner can't resume from the repo side:**
- The runner the cards name, `run_chain_A.py`, is VM-only and not in this repo (recipe C, recipe J). There is no
  `run_chain_s0v32.py` in the repo either. Its options and its handling of partial outputs can't be read or changed
  here.
- Nothing in the repo verified a stage's completion. `chain_reuse.record` takes `total_cost.txt` as "solved", but
  `switch_model.reporting` writes that file before the later post-solve steps, `prepare_next_stage` (the handover)
  last among them.

**`s0_workflow/scripts/resume_chain.py` (new).** It runs before the chain runner, as `reuse_chain_stages.py` does. It
reads the chain's scenarios file.

`status` (read only) reports each stage as COMPLETE, PARTIAL or NOT_STARTED. COMPLETE requires all of these:
1. `model_config.json` (Switch writes it after the solver returns; an infeasible solve raises first) and a numeric
   `total_cost.txt`;
2. the results carried forward: `BuildGen.csv`, plus, for a stage with a next stage, `BuildStorageEnergy.csv`,
   `SuspendGen.csv` and `BuildTx.csv`;
3. the handover: every `*.chained.<case>.csv` the next stage's line aliases is in the next stage's inputs, written
   after this stage's `total_cost.txt`. These are built and retired capacity, costs, transmission, headroom state, and
   the build-rate history. That last one comes from the solved model, so a stage without its handover must be solved
   again;
4. a solver log in the outputs folder, if there is one, says `optimal`;
5. if `chain_provenance.json` exists, the inputs still match its digest.

`resume`:
- **Where it starts:** at `--from-stage`, or by default at the first stage that isn't COMPLETE.
- **It refuses:**
  - to start at a stage unless every earlier stage is COMPLETE;
  - while the interrupted run's files are in place (outputs from the start stage on, chained inputs after it) unless
    `--move-partial`;
  - with `--inputs-not-after <UTC>`, if any built (non-chained) input file changed after that time.
- **What it writes and moves:**
  - It never writes built inputs, and it never calls `pg_to_switch.py`.
  - `--move-partial` moves files (never deletes them) to `<outputs root>/_resume_partial_<UTC>/`.
  - It writes `scenarios_<case>[.fixed].from_<stage>.txt` for the chain runner, and a `resume` row in
    `handoff_runs.csv`.

**`s0_workflow/runcards/v32/RESUME.md`:** a runner-ready card with these steps:
- the read-only assessment (`status`, then `resume --dry-run --inputs-not-after 2026-10-14T02:42Z`);
- verifying each stage: the files above, plus the solve log's `Optimization termination condition was optimal` and
  `Switch completed successfully`;
- moving the partial outputs aside;
- the resume command, and the chain runner on the remaining lines;
- recording the reference at 4894d6a once all five stages are COMPLETE;
- the `READY_SCENARIOS` queue, in order;
- a STOP list.

**Tests** (`test_resume_chain.py`, a synthetic three-stage chain):
- every state is classified correctly;
- the VM-like case: 2028 complete, 2030 interrupted in its post-solve with no handover and a half-written chained
  file. The resume starts at 2030, refuses to start at 2035, and refuses without `--move-partial`. It moves 2030's
  outputs and 2035's chained inputs aside, keeps 2030's chained inputs and 2028's outputs, and never changes a built
  input;
- a non-optimal termination, and inputs changed since recorded;
- the rebuilt-input guard;
- the command line.

**READY files:**
- Re-issued at this commit, because code was added. The added code is `s0_workflow/scripts/`, which the reuse code
  check exempts, along with tests, cards and `*.md`. So this commit runs the same build and model code as 4894d6a.
- **It is a resume, not a rebuild:** the 02:40 inputs stand (RESUME.md, the README).
- Stages solved before and after the shutdown share 4894d6a's code; the reference is recorded at 4894d6a.

**F3:** unaffected; no build or model code changed. **SHARED_CHANGES:** no row; row 133 stays free.

## 244. S0 v3.2 Final: Two Offshore Fleet Fixes Not Applied (Not Data-Only); Logged as v4 Items (Bucket B)

**Date:** 2026-10-14. **Branch:** `tom/s0-v3.2`. **Numbering:** the S0 v3.2 final fixes planned as §244–§247 move to
§245–§248.

**Tom's rule:** apply only if the change is data-only and doesn't delay the READY files; otherwise log it as a v4
bucket B item (supply inputs).

### v4 item B-1: Coastal Virginia Offshore Wind (CVOW), about 1.9 GW missing

- **Fleet now:** zone p99 (VA) has CVOW's 12 MW pilot (2021) and 713 MW in 2027. The full commercial project is about
  2,600 MW (BOEM, `pg/extra_inputs/offshore_wind/boem_projects_jan2025.csv`: OCS-A 0483, COP approved 31 Oct 2023,
  under construction).
- **Why it can't go in as data:**
  - The fleet comes from PowerGenome's EIA-860M read (`eia_860m_fn: july_generator2025.xlsx`, statuses V, TS, U, T).
  - PowerGenome's settings here have no way to add a planned unit: only `additional_retirements`.
  - Adding the missing capacity would need either code (a fleet-addition hook like the coal spec's) or an edited 860M
    spreadsheet. The spreadsheet is shared data on the VM, which the shared-paths rule forbids editing in place, and
    every case reads it.
- **Does it meet the inclusion rule?** Probably yes: the project is physically under construction (BOEM; the developer,
  Dominion Energy, reports offshore installation under way), which is status U or V. But the July 2025 860M evidently
  lists only 713 MW of it under an included status.
- **To check on the VM before v4:** in `july_generator2025.xlsx` (and the latest 860M vintage), the CVOW generators'
  IDs, MW, status codes and planned operation dates. If a later vintage lists all of it as U or V, the fix is a v4
  860M vintage update, which affects every plant and needs its own bridge check.
- **Sources to cite then:** the EIA-860M row(s); BOEM's CVOW commercial page (the CSV's `source` column); Dominion
  Energy's CVOW project page and quarterly filings for the schedule.

### v4 item B-2: Sunrise Wind (924 MW, 2027) in p131 (MA), landing on Long Island

- **The landing point:** the export cable lands on Long Island (Suffolk County) and connects at Holbrook. That is
  NYISO zone K, ReEDS zone **p128** (NY), the zone that already holds South Fork. The target zone is clear.
- **Why it can't go in as data:**
  - The zone comes from `pg/extra_inputs/reeds_plant_map.csv` (EIA plant ID → zone; `plant_region_map_fn`). The fix
    is one row, but Sunrise Wind's EIA plant ID is only in the 860M file on the VM; nothing in the repo identifies it.
  - That map is also read by the v3.1 and flags-off builds, so editing it in place would break F3's certification.
- **The v4 fix:** a v3.2-only copy of the map with that plant's row set to p128, selected by
  `s0_production.settings.plant_region_map_fn` (settings and data, no code), and reverted in the flags-off patch. It
  needs the plant ID from the VM's 860M (look up "Sunrise Wind").
- **Effect until then:** 924 MW of offshore output and capacity sits in New England (p131) instead of Long Island
  (p128) from the 2028 stage.

**Not changed:** code, settings, the fleet. The READY files are unaffected.

## 245. S0 v3.2 Final: No New Nuclear in California; Other States' Restrictions (v4 Item, State Policy)

**Date:** 2026-10-15. **Branch:** `tom/s0-v3.2`.

**Why:** the S0 v3.2 bridge built 9.6 GW (v3.1) and 12.9 GW (v3.2 pre) of new nuclear in California by 2045. California
bars new nuclear: Public Resources Code §25524.1–25524.2. Its condition (a federally approved high-level waste
disposal technology) is unmet, so in effect it is a ban.

### The rule

`s0_production.state_nuclear_bans: {states: [CA]}`, implemented in `production.write_state_nuclear_bans`.
- **What it adds:** a maximum-capacity program per state, `MaxCapTag_StateBan_<ST>_Nuclear`, at 0 MW in every period.
- **What it covers:** that state's new-build nuclear candidates: `gen_tech` `Nuclear_*` (the ATB names) in its zones
  (from the state column of `hierarchy.csv`; California is p8, p9, p10 and p11), with no predetermined build in the
  stage.
- **Existing plants are never listed** (Diablo Canyon, `gen_tech` `Nuclear`). `max_capacity_constraint` counts every
  build of a listed project active in the period, so listing one would make the 0 MW cap infeasible. The build stops if
  a listed project has predetermined capacity.
- **Files:** it appends to `max_cap_requirements.csv` and `max_cap_generators.csv`, and writes `state_nuclear_bans.csv`
  plus a log line. A stage that offers no new nuclear (before 2035) adds nothing and reports that.
- **Flag:** ON in the v3.2 defaults; `[]` = v3.1, the code default. Off in the flags-off patch and pinned off in the
  regression row (`on_pgdays`; SHARED_CHANGES row 133).

**Tests** (`test_state_nuclear_bans.py`):
- California's new-build candidates are capped at 0, and Diablo Canyon, Washington and solar are not;
- existing programs are kept, and a second run is refused;
- with the flag off the files are byte-identical;
- a stage without new nuclear adds nothing;
- a predetermined candidate is refused;
- the step is wired into the case-input steps.

### v4 item (state policy): other states' restrictions on new nuclear, not modelled in v3.2

Tom's rule: California only in v3.2. Outright bans and federal-repository conditions are candidates for v4. Voter or
legislative approval conditions are **not** to be modelled as bans.

**Overall source:** NCSL, *States Restrictions on New Nuclear Power Facility Construction*, updated 16 Apr 2026. It
lists ten states: CA, CT, HI, ME, MA, MN, NY, OR, RI and VT; MN outright; NY outright in a limited area; waste-disposal
conditions in CA, CT, ME and OR; legislative approval in HI, MA, RI and VT; voter approval in CT, ME, MA and OR.
NCSL's page could not be fetched from this environment, so the citations below come from the states' codes and the
reporting listed, and the "Verified" column says how far each was checked.

| State | Citation | Current status (Oct 2026) | Condition type | v4 candidate? | Verified |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| CA | Pub. Res. Code §25524.1–25524.2 | In force. AB 2647 (2026) was amended from an exemption into a CEC study (report due July 2027) | federal-repository condition (in effect a ban) | **modelled in v3.2** | statute and AB 2647 history (ANS, Feb 2026) |
| MN | Minn. Stat. §216B.243 subd. 3b | In force since 1994. 2025–26 repeal bills (SF 350, SF 468, HF 2670) not enacted; HF 2670 dead 18 May 2026 | **outright ban** (no certificate of need for new nuclear) | **yes** (Tom) | statute table on revisor.mn.gov; Senate counsel memo |
| NY | Pub. Auth. Law §1020-t (LIPA Act) | In force | **outright ban, limited area**: LIPA may not build or operate nuclear in its service area (Nassau, Suffolk, part of Queens; §1020-a) | **yes, for that area** (model zone p128, Long Island) | statute text (NY Senate) |
| OR | ORS 469.595; voter approval ORS 469.597 | In force. 2025 SB 215 (repeal, then an SMR exemption, then a study) not shown enacted | **federal-repository condition** (no site certificate until a federally licensed high-level waste repository exists), plus voter approval | **yes** (Tom), on the repository condition | statute text quoted in 2025 committee documents |
| CT | C.G.S. §22a-136 | In force with exemptions: sites of plants operating on 1 Oct 2022 (P.A. 22-76, 23-102); advanced reactors under P.A. 25-173 conditions | waste-disposal condition, plus voter/legislative approval (as NCSL classifies) | possible, but the exemptions cover Millstone and advanced reactors | 2026 statute supplement history |
| ME | NCSL lists a waste-disposal condition and voter approval | Listed by NCSL as in force | federal-repository condition plus voter approval | possible | **citation not verified** (38 M.R.S. §1493 covers low-level waste sites, not reactors) |
| HI | Const. Art. XI §8 | Listed by NCSL as in force | legislative approval (two-thirds of each house) | no (approval type) | citation from NCSL's table (2019 mirror) |
| MA | 1982 ballot law (St. 1982) | In force; repeal bill in committee (NCSL, Apr 2026) | voter and legislative approval | no (approval type) | **citation not verified** |
| RI | NCSL lists legislative approval | Listed by NCSL as in force | legislative approval | no (approval type) | **citation not verified** |
| VT | 30 V.S.A. §248 | Listed by NCSL as in force; repeal bill pending (NCSL, Apr 2026) | legislative approval | no (approval type) | **citation not verified** |
| NJ | CAFRA amendment, S3870 | **Lifted:** signed 8 Apr 2026. Waste storage now judged safe by DEP instead of requiring a federal repository | (was a federal-repository condition) | **no: repealed** | Governor's office, 8 Apr 2026; NJ Monitor; ANS |
| IL | SB 25 | **Lifted:** signed 8 Jan 2026, effective 1 Jun 2026 | (was a waste condition) | no | ANS; CATF |
| WI, KY, MT, WV | — | Lifted (2016, 2017, 2021, 2022) | — | no | NCSL summary |

**Change from Tom's note:** New Jersey (flagged as a federal-repository state) lifted its condition in April 2026, so
it is no longer a candidate. The federal-repository candidates are OR (and possibly ME and CT, pending their citations
and exemptions). The outright-ban candidates are MN and NY's LIPA area.

**To do in v4, before modelling any of these:**
- verify the unverified citations against the current codes (or NCSL's page);
- map each state, or for New York the LIPA area, to model zones;
- reuse `state_nuclear_bans` (it takes any state list). New York's area ban needs a zone list rather than a state.

**Sources:**
- NCSL (16 Apr 2026): https://www.ncsl.org/environment-and-natural-resources/states-restrictions-on-new-nuclear-power-facility-construction
- Minnesota Revisor: https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/table2?full=216B.243
- Minnesota HF 2670: https://www.billtrack50.com/billdetail/1875647
- NY Pub. Auth. Law §1020-t: https://nysenate.gov/legislation/laws/PBA/1020-T
- Oregon 2025 SB 215 committee documents: https://apps.oregonlegislature.gov/liz/2025R1/Downloads/CommitteeMeetingDocument/292781
- Connecticut ch. 446: https://www.cga.ct.gov/2024/sup/chap_446.htm
- New Jersey: https://www.nj.gov/governor/news/2026/approved/20260408a.shtml
- Illinois: https://www.ans.org/news/article-7664/illinois-lifts-moratorium-on-new-large-nuclear-reactors/
- California AB 2647: https://www.ans.org/news/2026-02-24/article-7793/california-bill-looks-to-craft-advanced-nuclear-exception-to-moratorium/

## 246. S0 v3.2 Final: Load and Hydro Budgets to the Full Record (`cf_fidelity.load_hydro`)

**Date:** 2026-10-15. **Branch:** `tom/s0-v3.2`.

**Why (the S0 v3.2 bridge):** the day weights let national annual load drift by up to ±1.5%:
- v3.2 against v3.1: +19 / 0 / +1 / +49 / +48 TWh by stage;
- v3.2 against the full record: +5 / 0 / +1 / +33 / +21 TWh.

The hydro budgets weren't rescaled at all: −14 to +31 TWh.

**The fix:** `s0_production.time_sampling.cf_fidelity.load_hydro: rescale`, with `cf_fidelity.mode: rescale`.
- **Load:** each zone's sampled load is multiplied by (full-record mean ÷ sample-weighted mean). The scaling is uniform
  over the sampled hours, the peak day included (Tom, 15 Oct 2026). So each zone's weighted annual load equals the
  full record's.
- **Hydro:** each project's sampled flow is rescaled the same way (`gens.HYDRO == 1`).
  - The cap is the column's full-record maximum, at least 1, because PowerGenome's `hydro_factor` (2 here) lifts hydro
    profiles above 1. The energy the cap removes is put back on the uncapped hours, as for wind and solar.
  - The hydro budgets (`hydro_avg_flow_mw`, the per-timeseries mean) and the water-node flows follow from the rescaled
    profiles.
- **Untouched:** the PRM stress days (weight 0). The day selection is unchanged, as are the timepoints and the model
  size, so there is no added solve time.
- **Where it runs:** in `pg_to_switch.operational_files`, right after the timeseries are fixed, before the hydro tables
  and the loads.
- **Report:** `load_hydro_fidelity.csv` (per zone and hydro project: full-record and sampled mean before and after,
  scale, residual, capped share, full-record annual value), and a log line with the national TWh moved.
- **The shared code change:** `cf_fidelity._rescale_column`'s cap became a parameter, defaulting to 1.0, so the
  wind/solar rescale computes exactly as before.

**Flag:**
- ON in the v3.2 defaults.
- Off in the code default, which is also what an absent key means.
- In the flags-off patch it goes off with `cf_fidelity` (the line returns to v3.1's).
- The regression row (`on_pgdays`) already sets `cf_fidelity: {mode: "off"}`, so no new pin is needed.

**Tests** (`test_load_hydro_fidelity.py`):
- load and hydro match the full record;
- the load scaling is uniform per zone, the peak day included;
- hydro above 1 isn't clipped at 1;
- stress days and other columns are untouched;
- the hydro budget Switch reads sums to the full record;
- the settings: v3.2 ON, the code default off, needs `rescale`, rejects an unknown value; the regression row is off;
- the wind/solar rescale gives byte-identical results to F3's code (0c9d9f7);
- the step is wired before the hydro tables and the loads.

**F3:** with `cf_fidelity` off (the flags-off patch) the step doesn't run, and the shared function computes as before.

## 247. S0 v3.2 Final: Build-Rate Package ON (R1, R11, R4, R2, R7); Two-Stage Test Card

**Date:** 2026-10-15. **Branch:** `tom/s0-v3.2`.

**Decision (Tom):** the build-rate package (§121–§125), approved for v3.2, goes ON in the S0 v3.2 base and in all seven
scenario cases. S3 and S5 have no build rate, so they are unaffected. It runs only after the two-stage test passes.

**Why R1 matters (the bridge):** the costlier wind connection (§126) cut 2028–30 wind, and the ramp, based on the
model's own past build, then locked in 11.5 GW less wind in every later stage. R1 bases the ramp on max(the chained
build, the previous stage's data R).

### Settings: `s0_production.settings.build_rate` (S0 cases only; `build_rate.yml` is unchanged)

| Key | v3.2 | v3.1 |
|---|---|---|
| `ramp_base` (R1) | `max_build_r` | `build` |
| `stage_r` (R11) | `max_data_prev_build` | `data` |
| `ramp_floor` (R4) | `reeds_gbin_min` (100 MW per state × 48 = 4,800 MW/yr per group) | `placeholder` (1,000) |
| `handover` (R2) | `linear` | `off` |
| `construction_completion` (R7) | `lbnl_post_ia` (0.722) | `placeholder` (0.9) |
| `tables_dir` | `build_rate/outputs_v32` | `build_rate/outputs` |

- **The tables:** R2 and R7 need tables made with the same flags: `python -m brc.cli run --outputs outputs_v32 --set
  near_term.handover.mode=linear --set near_term.construction_completion_source=lbnl_post_ia`. The case build checks
  `run_info.yaml`. `build_rate/outputs*` is gitignored, so the VM makes `outputs_v32` (the card's step 1).
- **Checked here:** the same command on the repo's raw files writes all eight levels with those flags. The expected
  stage R is in `s0_workflow/data/build_rate_v32/stage_r.csv` (column `r2r7`).

### Flags-off and regression row

- **The flags-off patch** (`v32_flags_off.patch`) now makes **16 changes**: §245 added 1 line, §246 changed
  `cf_fidelity`'s line, and §247 added 6 lines (the package back to v3.1). The patched yml still equals the flags-off
  commit (4696653), apart from keys pinned at their v3.1 values. `flagsoff_build2.md` is updated.
- **The regression row (`on_pgdays`)** pins the package at v3.1 and sets `cf_fidelity.load_hydro: "off"`
  (SHARED_CHANGES row 134). It resolves to v3.1's settings: `test_regression_row_resolves_as_before_the_one_column_fix`
  lists every pinned key, and `test_legacy_settings_build_as_before` still builds byte-identical files.

### Tests updated deliberately

- `test_v32_run_cards_cases_on_the_v32_base`: all seven cases, all five stages: the package ON (`v32_flags`) and
  `tables_dir` `outputs_v32`; also the CA ban and `load_hydro`.
- `test_scenario_management_differs_only_by_the_regression_row_v31_pins` and the S-set resolution: the new pins and
  flags.
- `test_v32_flags_off_patch.py`: 16 lines; the new `test_br_twostage_isolation_patch_turns_off_load_hydro_only`.

### The two-stage test card (`s0_workflow/runcards/v32/br_twostage.md`)

- **Case:** S0prod_A, 2028 and 2030, at the READY commit, into `switch/in/s0prod_A_v32brtest`.
- **Compared with:** S0 v3.2 pre (`s0prod_A_v32`, 4894d6a).
- **Isolation:** `s0_workflow/data/v32/br_twostage_isolation.patch` (not committed) turns `load_hydro` off. The CA
  nuclear ban is inert before 2035.

  - the options are R1/R11 with divisor 1.3;
  - the ramp floor is 4,800;
  - R per stage matches `stage_r.csv` within 1%: 2028 solar 36,289 (R7); 2030 solar 30,414 and storage 16,615 (R2).
- **Pass criteria:**
  - both stages complete and optimal, with the R1/R11 columns chained;
  - 2030 wind has no binding ramp (R1);
  - R11's cap is at least the data R;
  - 2030 wind, solar and storage builds are at least pre's, and 2028 solar falls by at most 2.2% (R7);
  - no new slack.
- **If any fails:** STOP; S0 v3.2 final doesn't run.

**SHARED_CHANGES:** row 134.

## 249. S0 v3.2 Final: Diablo Canyon Retires After the 2030 Stage (Current California Law); v4 Sensitivity Logged

**Date:** 2026-10-15. **Branch:** `tom/s0-v3.2`.

**Before (S0 v3.1 and S0 v3.2 pre): no retirement at all.**
- Diablo Canyon (EIA plant 6099, zone p9) is `p9_nuclear_1`: 1,122 MW built in 1985 and 1,118 MW in 1986, with
  `gen_max_age` 500 and `gen_can_retire_early` 0 (committed fedpol inputs; same PowerGenome settings and EIA-860M).
- The build years are the real ones, not a planned retirement encoded as year − 500, so the EIA-860M PowerGenome reads
  gives it no planned retirement date.
- No S0 rule touches nuclear: the lifetime backstop, retirement rule and friction cover coal and gas only.
- So Diablo ran to 2045 in every stage and couldn't retire early.

**California law (Tom):**
- SB 846 (2022) authorises operation to 2029 (Unit 1) and 2030 (Unit 2).
- NRC renewed the licences to 2044/2045 (April 2026). Operating past 2030 still needs the Legislature, a Water Board
  once-through-cooling exemption (deadline Oct 2030) and a new State Lands lease (the current one ends 31 Oct 2030).

### The change (settings only)

> **Superseded by §250:** `additional_retirements` broke every build (PowerGenome applies it to the EIA-860m
> proposed table too, which has no `plant_id_eia` column). §250 sets the date in the case writer instead.

- **The retirement:** `s0_production.settings.additional_retirements: [[6099, 1, 2031], [6099, 2, 2031]]`. These are
  PowerGenome's `[plant_id_eia, generator_id, year]` entries, merged into S0 cases only; `resources.yml` keeps `~`.
- **Why 2031 rather than SB 846's 2029/2030:**
  - pg_to_switch encodes a planned retirement year Y as build year Y − 500, with `gen_max_age` 500.
  - Under Switch's default `retire_time: late`, a build runs in a period if it came online at or before the period's
    start and retires after it.
  - The 2030 stage starts in 2029 and the 2035 stage in 2031. So 2031 keeps both units in service through the 2030
    stage and out from 2035, as Tom asked ("retire after the 2030 stage").
  - With SB 846's own years, Unit 1 (2029) would already be gone from the 2030 stage.
- **The check:** `s0_production.retirement_checks: [{name: Diablo Canyon, project: p9_nuclear_1, last_period: 2030}]`,
  implemented in `production.write_retirement_checks`.
  - It computes the project's MW in service per period with Switch's rule, writes `retirement_checks.csv` and a log
    line, and stops the build if the plant is absent in a period up to 2030 or present after.
  - A case without zone p9 (regional scope) isn't checked; that is reported.
- **Flags:** ON in v3.2. The flags-off patch sets both back to v3.1 (`~` and `[]`); it now makes **18 changes**. The
  regression row pins both (SHARED_CHANGES row 135).
- **Cards:**
  - `s0_v32.md`: a settings-table row, and pre-solve check 15. Before solving: 2,240 MW in 2028 and 2030, 0 from 2035.
    After the 2030 solve: the 2035 chained predetermined builds have no Diablo build in service. After the 2035 solve:
    no capacity.
  - The scenario cards point to items 10–15.

**Tests** (`test_retirement_checks.py`):
- the settings: v3.2 ON, the code default and the regression row off;
- Diablo in service in 2028 and 2030, and gone in 2035 whether encoded or left out;
- the v3.1 dates (1985/86) stop a 2035 build;
- SB 846's own years would drop Unit 1 from the 2030 stage;
- missing in 2028 stops the build;
- a regional case without p9 is skipped;
- the check is wired into the case-input steps.

Also updated: the flags-off patch test (18 lines), the regression-row resolution and the run-card case test (the
Diablo settings in all seven cases).

### The two-stage build-rate test still certifies

A test run at 5d2e60a certifies the final run at this commit. In 2028 and 2030, the only change is Diablo's encoded
build years (1531 instead of 1985/1986, the same 2,240 MW in service) and the new `retirement_checks.csv`. Neither
touches the build-rate package or its pass criteria. `br_twostage.md` says so.

### v4 item (state policy, sensitivity): "Diablo Canyon extended to 2044/45 (NRC licence)"

- **What it would represent:** Diablo running to its renewed NRC licence terms, 2044 (Unit 1) and 2045 (Unit 2). That
  assumes the Legislature extends SB 846, the Water Board grants the once-through-cooling exemption, and State Lands
  renews the lease.
- **How:** a sensitivity axis value that sets `additional_retirements` to [6099, 1, 2045] and [6099, 2, 2046] and
  `retirement_checks` to match. By the same encoding both units are then in service through the 2045 stage, which
  starts in 2041; the model ends there. The same keys, so no code is needed.

**SHARED_CHANGES:** row 135.

## 250. S0 v3.2 Final: Diablo Canyon's Retirement Set by the Case Writer (§249 Broke Every Build); VM Smoke Gate

**Date:** 2026-10-15. **Branch:** `tom/s0-v3.2`.

**The failure (VM):** §249 broke every build, with `KeyError: 'plant_id_eia'` in PowerGenome's `label_retirement_year`.
- PowerGenome applies `additional_retirements` to the proposed (EIA-860m) generators too. That table keeps
  `plant_id_eia` in its index, not as a column.
- The list had always been empty before §249, so that code path had never run.
- **My error:** I sent §249 as "settings only" with no VM build. Cloud tests can't catch it: the cloud has no
  PowerGenome data, and the PowerGenome clone is off limits here.

### The change: the case writer sets the date (PowerGenome untouched)

- **Removed:** `s0_production.settings.additional_retirements`, from `s0_production.yml`, the flags-off patch, the
  regression row (`on_pgdays`) and the tests. PowerGenome now reads exactly what it read at 5d2e60a (CHANGES §248).
- **The setting:** the existing flag, extended: `s0_production.retirement_checks: [{name: Diablo Canyon, project:
  p9_nuclear_1, last_period: 2030, retire_year: 2031, expected_mw: 2240}]`.
  - ON in v3.2. `[]` (the code default) in v3.1, in the flags-off patch and in the regression row.
  - `retire_year` must be after `last_period`.
- **The step:** `production.apply_retirements`, the **first** step of `write_case_inputs`, so it runs before any step
  that reads `gen_max_age` (`prm`'s existing capacity, the check).
  - For each entry with `retire_year`, it sets the project's `gen_info.csv` `gen_max_age` to `retire_year` minus its
    latest predetermined build year.
  - For Diablo: 2031 − 1986 = **45**. Unit 1 (1985) retires 2030 and Unit 2 (1986) 2031. Under Switch's rule (online ≤
    period start < online + `gen_max_age`), both are in service in the periods starting 2026 and 2029 (the 2028 and
    2030 stages) and neither from 2031 (2035 on).
  - `gen_info.csv` is read as text and written back, so "." and every other value are kept. That is the pattern of
    the coal and retirement-rule steps.
  - It writes `retirements_applied.csv` (build years, `gen_max_age` before and after) and a log line.
  - A stage without the project is reported, not changed. A `retire_year` not after the latest build year stops the
    build.
  - One `gen_max_age` per project, so an older build retires earlier. That is harmless here: both units are in
    service through 2030.
- **Costs:** Diablo's overnight cost is 0, so the change in capital-recovery factor (`crf` with age 45) changes
  nothing. Fixed O&M is paid only while a build is in service.
- **The check:** `write_retirement_checks` (§249) now also checks `expected_mw` (±1 MW). It catches a cluster that
  picked up another plant: 2,240 MW in 2028 and 2030, 0 MW from 2035.
- **The 2035 handover:**
  - The 2030 solve carries Diablo's 1985 and 1986 builds into 2035's chained `gen_build_predetermined`, as it carries
    every existing build.
  - 2035's own `gen_info.csv` (`gen_max_age` 45) retires them, so neither is in service: 0 MW and no fixed O&M.
  - Removing the project from the 2035 files instead would mean editing every file that names it (costs, heat rates,
    reserves, policy lists) and the chained files written at solve time. I judged that too risky for this run.
  - **New script:** `s0_workflow/scripts/check_retirements.py <stage inputs> --case S0prod_A [--outputs <stage
    outputs>]`. It checks the built and chained predetermined builds with the stage's `gen_info`, and `gen_cap.csv`.
    Exit 1 on any failure. Card check 15 runs it after the 2030 solve and after each later solve.

**Tests:**
- `test_retirement_checks.py`, rewritten:
  - the settings (v3.2 ON, no `additional_retirements`; the regression row and the code default off);
  - `gen_max_age` 500 → 45, with the gas plant and "." untouched;
  - 2,240 MW in 2028 and 2030, 0 in 2035 and 2040;
  - the chained 2035 file and `gen_cap.csv` pass, and with age 500 they fail;
  - the v3.1 date stops a 2035 build, and a wrong MW stops the build;
  - SB 846's own year (2029) drops both units from 2030;
  - a `retire_year` before a build stops the build;
  - the regional skip;
  - off (`{}`, `[]`, or an entry without `retire_year`) leaves `gen_info.csv` byte-identical;
  - the step runs first, before `prm` and the check.
- The flags-off patch test (now **17** changes), `test_s_set_on_v32.py` (all seven cases) and the regression-row test
  are updated.
- The flags-off and isolation patches are regenerated.

### The rule from now on: a VM smoke build before any full run (`s0_v32.md` section 2a; the run-card README)

- **When:** any change to what PowerGenome reads, or to the case writer.
- **What:** build only 2028 and 2035 into a scratch root (`switch\in\s0prod_A_v32smoke`), then run all pre-solve
  checks, `retirement_checks.csv` and `check_retirements.py` included. No solve.
- **The gate:** S0 v3.2 final starts only if it passes.
- **Time:** about 45–60 min, 1.5 h at most. The five-stage build takes about 83 min (§110), mostly per stage; the
  cache is warm.

### F3 and the two-stage test still certify

- **Flags-off (F3):** with `retirement_checks: []`, `apply_retirements` and the check return before reading anything.
  - The patched `s0_production.yml` parses to exactly 4696653's settings, apart from the known record-only keys
    (`test_v32_flags_off_patch.py`).
  - `additional_retirements` is gone from both sides, so the flags-off build reads what F3 read.
- **The two-stage test at 5d2e60a:**
  - `git diff 5d2e60a` on everything a build reads touches only `production.py`'s retirement functions, the one
    `retirement_checks` line in `s0_production.yml`, the regression row's `retirement_checks: []` and the two patches.
  - No build-rate code (`build_rate/`), Switch module or `pg_to_switch.py` changed.
  - In 2028 and 2030 the inputs differ from 5d2e60a's only in `p9_nuclear_1`'s `gen_max_age` (500 → 45, still in
    service at the same 2,240 MW) and the two new report files.
  - `br_twostage.md` says so.

### §249's v4 sensitivity, corrected

"Diablo Canyon extended to 2044/45 (NRC licence)": an axis value that sets the `retirement_checks` entry to
`last_period: 2045` with no `retire_year`, so the build years and age stay as PowerGenome writes them (in service to
2045). For an exact 2045 end, use `retire_year: 2046` (age 60: Unit 1 to 2045, Unit 2 to 2046). No PowerGenome
setting is involved.

**SHARED_CHANGES:** row 136.
